| **[A Mode-Unified, Continuous, and Biomimetic Control Strategy of a Robotic Lower Limb Prosthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19183v2)** | 2026-11-04 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Existing biomimetic lower-limb prosthesis controllers are commonly designed for discrete locomotion modes, requiring separate parameterizations for level walking, ramps, and stairs. Experimental results reproduced the principal slope-dependent biomechanical trends predicted by the unified model, with high agreement for knee-related measures (r-square = 0.82 - 0.99) and ankle kinematics (r-square= 0.78 - 0.96). These findings demonstrated that unified biomechanical parameterization enables a prosthesis control architecture across diverse terrain conditions while preserving key features of AB biomechanics without requiring independently tuned impedance parameter sets for each locomotion mode. Copyright may be transferred without notice, after which this version may no longer be accessible |
| **[Ab Initio Accuracy Beyond the Cutoff: Distributed Message-Passing Equivariant Attention at Tens-of-Millions-of-Atom Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06122v1)** | 2026-11-04 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Molecular dynamics predicts molecular motion from atomic forces and trajectory integration. Density functional theory (DFT) provides first-principles forces, but its $O(N^3)$ cost limits simulations to thousands of atoms; empirical force fields scale to millions but miss bond breaking and charge transfer. Equivariant message-passing Transformers learn the ab initio potential at linear cost. Stacked layers propagate information beyond a single cutoff, giving four layers a receptive field up to 23Å. At this frontier, scale is limited by communication: layers exchange boundary features across devices, force backpropagation repeats the exchange, and moving atoms change neighbor graphs and tensor shapes. We present Tessera, a distributed runtime that fixes workload shapes between rebuilds. It sorts atoms along a space-filling curve into equal-count shards, ensuring identical atom counts and tensor shapes per rank. Peer sets scale as $K^{0.5}$, not $K$. A conservative neighbor skin preserves topology across updates, enabling each rebuild interval to replay as one CUDA Graph. Fused kernels, three-level neighbor search, and overlapped All-to-All complete it. On 512 GPUs, Tessera sustains 30.7M atoms at 89.7% weak-scaling efficiency (77--90% across three per-GPU loads); per-step time rises 1.11--1.29x as machine and problem grow 16x. Graph reuse cuts full-step time $1.87\times$. Across an $8\times$ device increase, neighbor-search cost grows $1.7\times$, versus $3.0\times$ for Morton order. In a live $10.24$M-atom salt solution, NVE energy drift is $0.42\,μ$eV/atom/ps over $10$ ps, and structure matches a serial reference. A 10.06M-atom Norovirus capsid sustains 100 ps of NVT at $\approx 8$ ps/day on 256 A100 GPUs. | **[Agentic Behavior Engineering: A Design-by-Contract Methodology for Specifying, Verifying, and Governing Enterprise AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05454v1)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Enterprise teams that deploy large language model (LLM) agents inherit a maintenance problem that traditional testing does not address. ## structure from motion
| **Title** | **Date** | **Abstract** | **Comment** |

| **[Budget-aware Correspondence-driven View Selection for Real-time 3D Perception of Mobile Robots](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11809v1)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>3D visual perception and scene reconstruction are critical for autonomous robots and intelligent transportation, yet real-world deployments often generate long, redundant image sequences that hinder downstream processing. This report investigates BCVS (budget-aware correspondence-driven view sampling), a pose-free view selection algorithm that offers 22+ FPS on a CPU, enabling online filtering for efficient 3D perception by mobile robots. Unlike existing approaches that rely on full structure-from-motion (SfM) pipelines or camera pose estimation, BCVS employs a purely correspondence-based scoring strategy that prioritizes view novelty while maintaining sufficient overlap for geometric stability. Given a user-specified budget, BCVS iteratively selects the most distinct frames and suppresses near-duplicates, producing compact subsets that preserve connectivity and minimize reconstruction loss. Extensive experiments on benchmark show that BCVS outperforms CPU-friendly baselines at tight budgets and remains competitive as budgets scale. Field trials with large aerial and marine robotics data further validate its practicality and scalability: BCVS reduces redundancy up to 16% while sustaining robust SfM registration and high-quality 3D reconstructions.</p></details> | 12 pages |
| **[Towards Rotation-Only Imaging Geometry: Multi-View Pose Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11770v1)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Global structure-from-motion (SfM) commonly refines relative poses on individual image pairs and then estimates global rotations by rotation averaging. Pairwise refinement does not explicitly combine feature-level constraints with multi-view consistency during rotation initialization. We introduce Global Rotations from Aggregated Matches (GRAM), which estimates global rotations directly from initial relative rotations and inlier matches by minimizing edge-wise rotation-only algebraic residuals. GRAM replaces two-view relative-pose optimization and rotation averaging with a single stage. We further extend rotation- only imaging geometry to multiple views and develop Multi-view Optimization with Rotation-only Reprojection Error (MORE) for nonlinear global rotation refinement. Analytical Rotation-guided Translation (ART) derives locally scaled triplet translations from Linear Global Translation (LiGT) constraints and recovers global translations using weighted least squares. Synthetic and real-world experiments demonstrate the accuracy, robustness, and efficiency of the proposed methods.</p></details> |  |

| **[VAComp: View-Aware Full Projector Compensation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10637v1)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Full projector compensation adjusts a projector's input image to compensate for both geometric and photometric distortions introduced by the projection surface. To resolve the SfM scale ambiguity, we use metric calibration as a reference to recover the global scale; to calibrate the oppositely facing camera, we use two checkerboards, one facing the RGB camera and the other facing the depth camera. On 512 GPUs, Tessera sustains 30.7M atoms at 89.7% weak-scaling efficiency (77--90% across three per-GPU loads); per-step time rises 1.11--1.29x as machine and problem grow 16x. | **[Ground-Wing: A Parametric 3D Wing-in-Ground-Effect Dataset for Benchmarking Neural Surrogate Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04571v2)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This paper presents a CFD dataset of a highly parametrized 3D wing operating in ground effect, designed for benchmarking neural surrogate models for aerodynamic prediction, using a wing shape and flow conditions typically encountered in motorsport applications. The dataset is intended to be used as a challenging benchmark for surrogate CFD models, due to the uniqueness of the ground effect being absent from other wing and airfoil datasets. | **[SurgTAP-3D: A Large-Scale Cross-Surgery Video Dataset with Contact-Aware 3D Motion Tracking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11697v1)** | 2026-11-08 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Understanding 3D scene dynamics in minimally invasive surgery (MIS) is a fundamental step toward physically intelligent and autonomous surgical systems. This pipeline adapts foundation priors to surgical videos and produces temporally consistent trajectories, pseudo-scale geometry, camera poses, and instrument masks. Homepage: https://wrld.github.io/surgtap-3d/</p></details> | Accepted to IEEE Transactions on Robotics (T-RO) |

Project page: https://martine-benchmark.github.io/ |

| **[Ab Initio Accuracy Beyond the Cutoff: Distributed Message-Passing Equivariant Attention at Tens-of-Millions-of-Atom Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06122v1)** | 2026-11-04 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Molecular dynamics predicts molecular motion from atomic forces and trajectory integration. It sorts atoms along a space-filling curve into equal-count shards, ensuring identical atom counts and tensor shapes per rank. Peer sets scale as $K^{0.5}$, not $K$. A conservative neighbor skin preserves topology across updates, enabling each rebuild interval to replay as one CUDA Graph. Fused kernels, three-level neighbor search, and overlapped All-to-All complete it. On 512 GPUs, Tessera sustains 30.7M atoms at 89.7% weak-scaling efficiency (77--90% across three per-GPU loads); per-step time rises 1.11--1.29x as machine and problem grow 16x. Graph reuse cuts full-step time $1.87\times$. Across an $8\times$ device increase, neighbor-search cost grows $1.7\times$, versus $3.0\times$ for Morton order. In a live $10.24$M-atom salt solution, NVE energy drift is $0.42\,μ$eV/atom/ps over $10$ ps, and structure matches a serial reference. Finally, for random forests $F$ of any finite graph with arbitrary positive edge weights, we prove that \[ \Pr(e,f\in F)\le \frac{4}{3} \Pr(e\in F)\Pr(f\in F) \] for distinct edges $e,f$, improving the factor 2 supplied by general log-concavity bounds for matroid independent sets. | **[Ground-Wing: A Parametric 3D Wing-in-Ground-Effect Dataset for Benchmarking Neural Surrogate Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04571v2)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This paper presents a CFD dataset of a highly parametrized 3D wing operating in ground effect, designed for benchmarking neural surrogate models for aerodynamic prediction, using a wing shape and flow conditions typically encountered in motorsport applications. The dataset comprises 770 RANS simulations of 194 unique wing configurations, sampled across 27 airfoil profile variants and 4 distances from the ground, spanning variations in wing sweep, chord, taper, angle of attack, and tip twist. Six neural network architectures representing distinct paradigms --- convolutional (DoMINO), graph-based (X-MeshGraphNet), transformer-based (AB-UPT, GeoTransolver, GAOT), and JEPA-based (AeroJEPA) --- are evaluated on their ability to predict surface pressure coefficient and wall shear stress distributions, from which integral drag and lift coefficients are recovered. The dataset is intended to be used as a challenging benchmark for surrogate CFD models, due to the uniqueness of the ground effect being absent from other wing and airfoil datasets. The dataset is available for download at https://huggingface.co/datasets/andreacenzato/Ground-Wing</p></details> |  |

To address this gap, we introduce the RefRef dataset. It contains 150 synthetic and 60 real scenes spanning varying levels of geometric complexity and diverse background types that expose the limitations of existing methods. For the purpose of benchmarking, we also provide an oracle method that, given the object geometry and refractive indices, calculates accurate light paths for neural rendering, and a simple two-stage baseline that relaxes these assumptions. | **[GSPIM: A Temporal-Locality-Aware 4D Gaussian Splatting Accelerator with Polymorphic PIM and Multi-Frame Rendering Dataflow](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04804v3)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>4D Gaussian Splatting (4DGS) enables high-quality real-time novel-view synthesis for dynamic scenes, supporting free-viewpoint video, mobile XR, and autonomous systems at the edge. Multi-Frame Rendering Dataflow reuses active attributes and stable depth relationships within a temporal window, reducing repeated work across frames. By parameterizing weights as $W = AB^{\top}$ with $A \in \mathbb{R}^{m \times r}$, $B \in \mathbb{R}^{n \times r}$, we induce a posterior that is \emph{singular} with respect to the Lebesgue measure, concentrating on the rank-$r$ manifold. This singularity captures structured weight correlations through shared latent factors, geometrically distinct from mean-field's independence assumption. We derive PAC-Bayes generalization bounds whose complexity term scales as $\sqrt{r(m+n)}$ instead of $\sqrt{m n}$, and prove loss bounds that decompose the error into optimization and rank-induced bias using the Eckart-Young-Mirsky theorem. We further adapt recent Gaussian complexity bounds for low-rank deterministic networks to Bayesian predictive means. Empirically, across MLPs, LSTMs, and Transformers on standard benchmarks, our method achieves competitive predictive performance while using up to $33\times$ fewer parameters than 5-member Deep Ensembles. | **[Quo Vadis, Driving Scene Reconstruction? | **[Ab Initio Accuracy Beyond the Cutoff: Distributed Message-Passing Equivariant Attention at Tens-of-Millions-of-Atom Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06122v1)** | 2026-11-04 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Molecular dynamics predicts molecular motion from atomic forces and trajectory integration. Density functional theory (DFT) provides first-principles forces, but its $O(N^3)$ cost limits simulations to thousands of atoms; empirical force fields scale to millions but miss bond breaking and charge transfer. Equivariant message-passing Transformers learn the ab initio potential at linear cost. Stacked layers propagate information beyond a single cutoff, giving four layers a receptive field up to 23Å. At this frontier, scale is limited by communication: layers exchange boundary features across devices, force backpropagation repeats the exchange, and moving atoms change neighbor graphs and tensor shapes. We present Tessera, a distributed runtime that fixes workload shapes between rebuilds. It sorts atoms along a space-filling curve into equal-count shards, ensuring identical atom counts and tensor shapes per rank. Peer sets scale as $K^{0.5}$, not $K$. A conservative neighbor skin preserves topology across updates, enabling each rebuild interval to replay as one CUDA Graph. Fused kernels, three-level neighbor search, and overlapped All-to-All complete it. On 512 GPUs, Tessera sustains 30.7M atoms at 89.7% weak-scaling efficiency (77--90% across three per-GPU loads); per-step time rises 1.11--1.29x as machine and problem grow 16x. Graph reuse cuts full-step time $1.87\times$. Across an $8\times$ device increase, neighbor-search cost grows $1.7\times$, versus $3.0\times$ for Morton order. In a live $10.24$M-atom salt solution, NVE energy drift is $0.42\,μ$eV/atom/ps over $10$ ps, and structure matches a serial reference. A 10.06M-atom Norovirus capsid sustains 100 ps of NVT at $\approx 8$ ps/day on 256 A100 GPUs. Over 200,000 steps, it has no broken bonds or anomalously close pairs and uses three compiled variants, with no graph breaks or eager fallbacks.</p></details> |  |

| **[Agentic Behavior Engineering: A Design-by-Contract Methodology for Specifying, Verifying, and Governing Enterprise AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05454v1)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Enterprise teams that deploy large language model (LLM) agents inherit a maintenance problem that traditional testing does not address. ABE adapts Design by Contract to non-deterministic systems. A specification-only behavior contract with stable rule identifiers states what the agent must do, must not do, and when it must abstain for lack of evidence; a lightweight scenario library turns each rule into concrete situations; and every engineering artifact (tests, runtime policies, telemetry) references the contract in one direction only, so that specification and implementation can change independently without drifting apart. The method organizes the work into five responsibilities (Specify, Ground, Control, Verify, Observe), treats missing evidence and a verified negative as different things, enforces authority outside the model, gates releases on per-scenario pass^k reliability rather than aggregate accuracy, and classifies every production run against the contract so that each finding routes to the asset that must change. We prove local-to-global spectral-gap bounds for block Glauber dynamics. Given a symmetric, entrywise nonnegative matrix $A$ with zero diagonal and $λ_{\max}(A)&lt;1$ encoding local spectral bounds for a spin system, we derive global spectral-gap bounds for block dynamics on its marginal distributions. Our proof extends the Bochner method through an abstract inequality for orthogonal projections on Hilbert spaces. As an application, we prove that Glauber dynamics for the $O(N)$ model, with $N\geq 2$, arbitrary external fields, and couplings of either sign, has spectral gap at least $(1-\&#124;J_{\mathrm{abs}}\&#124;_{\mathrm{op}}/N)/n$, where $J$ is the interaction matrix and $J_{\mathrm{abs}}=(&#124;J_{ij}&#124;)$. We also obtain spectral bounds for swap walks and local spectral expansion of partite simplicial complexes. For complexes formed from disjoint groups of ranks in random flags of subspaces over $\mathbb F_q$, we obtain a one-sided local spectral expansion bound of $2\sqrt q/(q+1)$. This bound is independent of the number and sizes of each group, and is nontrivial for every prime power $q$. Finally, for random forests $F$ of any finite graph with arbitrary positive edge weights, we prove that \[ \Pr(e,f\in F)\le \frac{4}{3} \Pr(e\in F)\Pr(f\in F) \] for distinct edges $e,f$, improving the factor 2 supplied by general log-concavity bounds for matroid independent sets. The argument combines our extended Bochner method with the analysis of an auxiliary real stable polynomial.</p></details> | 42 pages |

Since generative compression in many methods treats geometric and color attributes via different parameters, it can induce dimension-specific distortions, motivating multi-dimensional assessment. ## 3D Gaussian Splatting
| **Title** | **Date** | **Abstract** | **Comment** |

| **[Plant Orbiter: 3D Plant Reconstruction and Generation with Video Diffusion Orbit Cameras and 3D Gaussian Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12739v1)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Modern digital phenotyping pipelines require the precise measurement of a range of 3D morphological plant traits. | **[Spaghetti Splatting: Differentiating Curvilinear Dirac Masses via Convolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12716v1)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>In production systems, 3D geometry is represented by primitives such as points, lines, and triangles, as rendering hardware is optimized to generate images from them very efficiently. As points and triangles have already received significant attention, we investigate this recipe for curvilinear primitives, and introduce Spaghetti Splatting. We approximate curves by polylines whose convolution converges to that of the continuous curve, and volume render each segment so that connected segments composite without overcounting at their joints. For fast training and inference, we linearize the camera projection per segment, which also accommodates kernels beyond Gaussians, such as Beta kernels. As a point is a curve of zero length, our representation is a strict superset of 3D Gaussian Splatting. It remains competitive with the state-of-the-art on general scenes, outperforms methods specialized to line-like geometry, natively supports anti-aliasing, and produces long connected strands that are directly amenable to physics simulation.</p></details> | 10 pages, 8 figures, 1 table |

From the data perspective, we review physical measurement campaigns, ray tracing simulation engines, and publicly available benchmark datasets, identifying their respective strengths and fundamental limitations. From the paradigm perspective, we establish a core taxonomy that categorizes RM construction into source-aware forward prediction and \added{source-agnostic} inverse reconstruction, and examine five principal neural architecture families spanning convolutional neural networks, vision transformers, graph neural networks, generative adversarial networks, and diffusion models. From the physics-awareness perspective, we introduce a three-level integration framework encompassing data-level feature engineering, loss-level partial differential equation regularization, and \added{architecture-level} structural isomorphism. The source code will be made publicly available. | **[Ab Initio Accuracy Beyond the Cutoff: Distributed Message-Passing Equivariant Attention at Tens-of-Millions-of-Atom Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06122v1)** | 2026-11-04 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Molecular dynamics predicts molecular motion from atomic forces and trajectory integration. Equivariant message-passing Transformers learn the ab initio potential at linear cost. Stacked layers propagate information beyond a single cutoff, giving four layers a receptive field up to 23Å. At this frontier, scale is limited by communication: layers exchange boundary features across devices, force backpropagation repeats the exchange, and moving atoms change neighbor graphs and tensor shapes. We present Tessera, a distributed runtime that fixes workload shapes between rebuilds. It sorts atoms along a space-filling curve into equal-count shards, ensuring identical atom counts and tensor shapes per rank. Peer sets scale as $K^{0.5}$, not $K$. A conservative neighbor skin preserves topology across updates, enabling each rebuild interval to replay as one CUDA Graph. Fused kernels, three-level neighbor search, and overlapped All-to-All complete it. On 512 GPUs, Tessera sustains 30.7M atoms at 89.7% weak-scaling efficiency (77--90% across three per-GPU loads); per-step time rises 1.11--1.29x as machine and problem grow 16x. Graph reuse cuts full-step time $1.87\times$. Across an $8\times$ device increase, neighbor-search cost grows $1.7\times$, versus $3.0\times$ for Morton order. A 10.06M-atom Norovirus capsid sustains 100 ps of NVT at $\approx 8$ ps/day on 256 A100 GPUs. Project page: https://svlwoof.github.io/gaussianrenderformer |
| **[Agentic Behavior Engineering: A Design-by-Contract Methodology for Specifying, Verifying, and Governing Enterprise AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05454v1)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Enterprise teams that deploy large language model (LLM) agents inherit a maintenance problem that traditional testing does not address. ABE adapts Design by Contract to non-deterministic systems. A specification-only behavior contract with stable rule identifiers states what the agent must do, must not do, and when it must abstain for lack of evidence; a lightweight scenario library turns each rule into concrete situations; and every engineering artifact (tests, runtime policies, telemetry) references the contract in one direction only, so that specification and implementation can change independently without drifting apart. The method organizes the work into five responsibilities (Specify, Ground, Control, Verify, Observe), treats missing evidence and a verified negative as different things, enforces authority outside the model, gates releases on per-scenario pass^k reliability rather than aggregate accuracy, and classifies every production run against the contract so that each finding routes to the asset that must change. Finally, for random forests $F$ of any finite graph with arbitrary positive edge weights, we prove that \[ \Pr(e,f\in F)\le \frac{4}{3} \Pr(e\in F)\Pr(f\in F) \] for distinct edges $e,f$, improving the factor 2 supplied by general log-concavity bounds for matroid independent sets. | **[3DGS-VBench+ and 3DGSV-Assessor: A Multi-Dimensional Subjective Benchmark and a Reference-Flexible Objective Model for 3D Gaussian Splatting Video Quality Assessment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04498v1)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) enables real-time novel view synthesis (NVS), but its heavy storage makes compression essential, and both training and compression introduce representation-specific distortions. ## 3DGS

| **[Mitigating SM-Level Load Imbalance in 3D Gaussian Splatting on GPUs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12319v1)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) enables photorealistic, real-time rendering on commodity GPUs and is increasingly adopted in generative world models and autonomous driving simulators. However, existing optimizations rely on algorithmic changes that often require retraining, or on specialized accelerators that lack flexibility across evolving 3DGS variants. In this paper, we identify a fundamental inefficiency in GPU-based 3DGS rendering: severe SM-level load imbalance caused by uneven workloads across screen tiles. This imbalance creates a long execution tail, leaving most SMs idle while a few process computationally intensive tiles, particularly in large-scale urban scenes. To address this, we present a software-defined GPU scheduling approach that mitigates the execution tail. Our approach uses a persistent kernel to schedule heavy tiles first and selectively chunks outlier tiles across multiple CTAs. A lightweight per-frame policy determines when and how much to chunk using tile statistics already available in the rendering pipeline. Our approach requires neither retraining nor specialized hardware and can be applied orthogonally to existing optimizations across diverse 3DGS variants. Averaged per scene, it accelerates alpha blending by up to 2.93x on a large-scale urban scene, and by up to 5.6x on its most imbalanced frames, while retaining the original alpha blending formulation.</p></details> |  |
| **[Ground-Wing: A Parametric 3D Wing-in-Ground-Effect Dataset for Benchmarking Neural Surrogate Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04571v2)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This paper presents a CFD dataset of a highly parametrized 3D wing operating in ground effect, designed for benchmarking neural surrogate models for aerodynamic prediction, using a wing shape and flow conditions typically encountered in motorsport applications. The dataset is intended to be used as a challenging benchmark for surrogate CFD models, due to the uniqueness of the ground effect being absent from other wing and airfoil datasets. The dataset is available for download at https://huggingface.co/datasets/andreacenzato/Ground-Wing</p></details> |  |
| **[DPGS-SLAM: Diffusion-Prior and Provenance-Aware Monocular Gaussian Splatting SLAM for Dynamic Visual Sensing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12038v1)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This paper presents DPGS-SLAM (Diffusion-Prior and Provenance-Aware Monocular Gaussian Splatting SLAM) for dense mapping in environments containing dynamic objects. Although 3DGS (3D Gaussian splatting) enables efficient dense mapping, existing 3DGS-SLAM systems may exhibit instability when dynamic objects violate their underlying static-scene assumption. | **[TryOnCrafter: Unleashing Camera Trajectories for Realistic Video Virtual Try-on via a Renderable 4D Try-on Proxy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26092v3)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>While Video Virtual Try-on (VVT) has achieved remarkable progress in synthesizing realistic garment overlays on dynamic subjects, existing paradigms remains fundamentally constrained by a passive dependency on source camera trajectories, failing to accommodate the requisite interactive freedom for omnidirectional viewpoint exploration. Specifically, we introduce Piecewise Perspective Rendering (PPR) to mitigate peripheral stretching through local perspective projections sharing a common camera center, and Spatial Affine Photometric Modeling (SAPM) to account for camera-dependent photometric variations during training. We further introduce **WideDrive**, a synthetic benchmark providing dense ground-truth wide views and adapt a LiDAR-projected sparse-reference protocol to real-world datasets. Extensive experiments on multiple datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of WV-GS for WVS. Dataset and code will be publicly released.</p></details> |  |

We present Tessera, a distributed runtime that fixes workload shapes between rebuilds. A conservative neighbor skin preserves topology across updates, enabling each rebuild interval to replay as one CUDA Graph. Fused kernels, three-level neighbor search, and overlapped All-to-All complete it. On 512 GPUs, Tessera sustains 30.7M atoms at 89.7% weak-scaling efficiency (77--90% across three per-GPU loads); per-step time rises 1.11--1.29x as machine and problem grow 16x. Graph reuse cuts full-step time $1.87\times$. Across an $8\times$ device increase, neighbor-search cost grows $1.7\times$, versus $3.0\times$ for Morton order. Project page: https://linus-franke.com/hibs/</p></details> | accepted to WACV 2027, project page: https://linus-franke.com/hibs/ |

| **[Ab Initio Accuracy Beyond the Cutoff: Distributed Message-Passing Equivariant Attention at Tens-of-Millions-of-Atom Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06122v1)** | 2026-11-04 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Molecular dynamics predicts molecular motion from atomic forces and trajectory integration. Density functional theory (DFT) provides first-principles forces, but its $O(N^3)$ cost limits simulations to thousands of atoms; empirical force fields scale to millions but miss bond breaking and charge transfer. Equivariant message-passing Transformers learn the ab initio potential at linear cost. Stacked layers propagate information beyond a single cutoff, giving four layers a receptive field up to 23Å. At this frontier, scale is limited by communication: layers exchange boundary features across devices, force backpropagation repeats the exchange, and moving atoms change neighbor graphs and tensor shapes. We present Tessera, a distributed runtime that fixes workload shapes between rebuilds. It sorts atoms along a space-filling curve into equal-count shards, ensuring identical atom counts and tensor shapes per rank. Peer sets scale as $K^{0.5}$, not $K$. A conservative neighbor skin preserves topology across updates, enabling each rebuild interval to replay as one CUDA Graph. Fused kernels, three-level neighbor search, and overlapped All-to-All complete it. On 512 GPUs, Tessera sustains 30.7M atoms at 89.7% weak-scaling efficiency (77--90% across three per-GPU loads); per-step time rises 1.11--1.29x as machine and problem grow 16x. Graph reuse cuts full-step time $1.87\times$. Across an $8\times$ device increase, neighbor-search cost grows $1.7\times$, versus $3.0\times$ for Morton order. In a live $10.24$M-atom salt solution, NVE energy drift is $0.42\,μ$eV/atom/ps over $10$ ps, and structure matches a serial reference. A 10.06M-atom Norovirus capsid sustains 100 ps of NVT at $\approx 8$ ps/day on 256 A100 GPUs. | **[3D Gaussian Splatting within Self-Learned Neural View-Dependent Color Fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03114v2)** | 2026-11-04 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) has become a vital tool in novel view synthesis. 3D Gaussian functions with attributes like color, opacity, and scale are stacked together to explicitly model a radiance field. To address this issue, we propose to learn a radiance field within a continuous view-dependent color field parameterized by neural networks. With continuous constraints in color, the bias on low-frequency signals of neural networks can highly encourage the geometry modeling to contribute to high-frequency color variations on images, getting rid of solely relying on the color attribute itself. Moreover, we impose a constraint to improve the Lipschitz continuity of the neural color field, making large changes of each Gaussian's color also match with large changes of its position in terms of a ratio. To this end, we additionally introduce novel self-learning strategies to improve the learning of the neural view-dependent color field without any external knowledge or priors, aiming for better generalization on unseen scenes. Our numerical and visual comparisons on widely used benchmarks justify our idea and show better ability of high fidelity geometry recovery in 3DGS. | **[GaussianRenderFormer: Transformer-based Rendering of 3D Gaussian Splats](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05593v1)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Can a neural network learn to render 3D Gaussian splats on its own, with no rasterizer in the renderer? Project page: https://svlwoof.github.io/gaussianrenderformer |

We also obtain spectral bounds for swap walks and local spectral expansion of partite simplicial complexes. | **[Ground-Wing: A Parametric 3D Wing-in-Ground-Effect Dataset for Benchmarking Neural Surrogate Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04571v2)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This paper presents a CFD dataset of a highly parametrized 3D wing operating in ground effect, designed for benchmarking neural surrogate models for aerodynamic prediction, using a wing shape and flow conditions typically encountered in motorsport applications. The dataset comprises 770 RANS simulations of 194 unique wing configurations, sampled across 27 airfoil profile variants and 4 distances from the ground, spanning variations in wing sweep, chord, taper, angle of attack, and tip twist. Six neural network architectures representing distinct paradigms --- convolutional (DoMINO), graph-based (X-MeshGraphNet), transformer-based (AB-UPT, GeoTransolver, GAOT), and JEPA-based (AeroJEPA) --- are evaluated on their ability to predict surface pressure coefficient and wall shear stress distributions, from which integral drag and lift coefficients are recovered. The dataset is intended to be used as a challenging benchmark for surrogate CFD models, due to the uniqueness of the ground effect being absent from other wing and airfoil datasets. The dataset is available for download at https://huggingface.co/datasets/andreacenzato/Ground-Wing</p></details> |  |
| **[Active Learning for Gaussian Process Regression Under Self-Induced Boltzmann Weights](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.10654v2)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We consider the active learning problem where the goal is to learn an unknown function with low prediction error under an unknown Boltzmann distribution induced by the function itself. This self-induced weighting arises naturally in problems such as potential energy surface (PES) modeling in computational chemistry, yet poses unique challenges as the target distribution is unknown and its partition function is intractable. We propose \texttt{AB-SID-iVAR}, a Gaussian Process-based acquisition function that approximates the intractable Bayesian target distribution in closed form while avoiding partition function estimation, and is applicable to both discrete and continuous input domains. We also analyze a Thompson sampling alternative (\texttt{TS-SID-iVAR}) as a higher variance Monte Carlo variant. Despite the unknown target, under mild conditions, we establish that the terminal prediction error vanishes with high probability, and provide a tighter average-case guarantee. We demonstrate consistent improvements over existing approaches in this setting on synthetic benchmarks and real-world PES modeling and drug discovery tasks. Our code is available at https://github.com/TsingQAQ/SIDAL</p></details> |  |
| **[Singular Bayesian Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.00387v5)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Bayesian neural networks promise calibrated uncertainty but require $O(mn)$ parameters for standard mean-field Gaussian posteriors. We argue this cost is often unnecessary, particularly when weight matrices exhibit fast singular value decay. By parameterizing weights as $W = AB^{\top}$ with $A \in \mathbb{R}^{m \times r}$, $B \in \mathbb{R}^{n \times r}$, we induce a posterior that is \emph{singular} with respect to the Lebesgue measure, concentrating on the rank-$r$ manifold. This singularity captures structured weight correlations through shared latent factors, geometrically distinct from mean-field's independence assumption. We derive PAC-Bayes generalization bounds whose complexity term scales as $\sqrt{r(m+n)}$ instead of $\sqrt{m n}$, and prove loss bounds that decompose the error into optimization and rank-induced bias using the Eckart-Young-Mirsky theorem. We further adapt recent Gaussian complexity bounds for low-rank deterministic networks to Bayesian predictive means. Empirically, across MLPs, LSTMs, and Transformers on standard benchmarks, our method achieves competitive predictive performance while using up to $33\times$ fewer parameters than 5-member Deep Ensembles. It substantially improves OOD detection and often improves calibration relative to mean-field and perturbation baselines, while Deep Ensembles can still be stronger on in-distribution likelihood-based metrics.</p></details> | 8 pages Main text, 53 pages Appendix, 20 figures Proceedings of the 43 rd International Conference on Machine Learning (ICML 2026) |
| **[Branch-Extended Automatic Differentiation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08637v1)** | 2026-11-05 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We present branch-extended automatic differentiation (BEAD), a software package that provides information about inactive branches of piecewise-differentiable functions, intended for use in nonsmooth optimization algorithms. BEAD is implemented in Python, building on the popular JAX framework. For supported nonsmooth operations such as abs and max, BEAD identifies alternative branches within tolerance from the given input, and computes gradients of the resulting branches extended to the given input. Such information has been previously used for robust optimization methods, but has required error-prone and tedious manual differentiation, unlike BEAD, which is fully automated for functions written in NumPy-like JAX code. | **[A Mode-Unified, Continuous, and Biomimetic Control Strategy of a Robotic Lower Limb Prosthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19183v2)** | 2026-11-04 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Existing biomimetic lower-limb prosthesis controllers are commonly designed for discrete locomotion modes, requiring separate parameterizations for level walking, ramps, and stairs. These findings demonstrated that unified biomechanical parameterization enables a prosthesis control architecture across diverse terrain conditions while preserving key features of AB biomechanics without requiring independently tuned impedance parameter sets for each locomotion mode. | **[Ab Initio Accuracy Beyond the Cutoff: Distributed Message-Passing Equivariant Attention at Tens-of-Millions-of-Atom Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06122v1)** | 2026-11-04 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Molecular dynamics predicts molecular motion from atomic forces and trajectory integration. Density functional theory (DFT) provides first-principles forces, but its $O(N^3)$ cost limits simulations to thousands of atoms; empirical force fields scale to millions but miss bond breaking and charge transfer. Equivariant message-passing Transformers learn the ab initio potential at linear cost. Stacked layers propagate information beyond a single cutoff, giving four layers a receptive field up to 23Å. At this frontier, scale is limited by communication: layers exchange boundary features across devices, force backpropagation repeats the exchange, and moving atoms change neighbor graphs and tensor shapes. We present Tessera, a distributed runtime that fixes workload shapes between rebuilds. It sorts atoms along a space-filling curve into equal-count shards, ensuring identical atom counts and tensor shapes per rank. Peer sets scale as $K^{0.5}$, not $K$. A conservative neighbor skin preserves topology across updates, enabling each rebuild interval to replay as one CUDA Graph. Fused kernels, three-level neighbor search, and overlapped All-to-All complete it. On 512 GPUs, Tessera sustains 30.7M atoms at 89.7% weak-scaling efficiency (77--90% across three per-GPU loads); per-step time rises 1.11--1.29x as machine and problem grow 16x. Graph reuse cuts full-step time $1.87\times$. Across an $8\times$ device increase, neighbor-search cost grows $1.7\times$, versus $3.0\times$ for Morton order. In a live $10.24$M-atom salt solution, NVE energy drift is $0.42\,μ$eV/atom/ps over $10$ ps, and structure matches a serial reference. A 10.06M-atom Norovirus capsid sustains 100 ps of NVT at $\approx 8$ ps/day on 256 A100 GPUs. Over 200,000 steps, it has no broken bonds or anomalously close pairs and uses three compiled variants, with no graph breaks or eager fallbacks.</p></details> |  |

| **[Data-driven candidate structure ensemble generation beyond the convex hull with crystALL approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02584v1)** | 2026-11-02 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Real materials consist of multiple stable or metastable states under real-world conditions, existing as statistical ergodic ensembles of configurations rather than ideal crystals. Yet, data-driven computational materials discovery increasingly biases itself toward finding "new stable structures" from ground-state DFT calculations alone. This limits our long-term ability to discover materials whose properties emerge from metastable phases, to explain experimental observations, or to model thermodynamic behavior comprehensively. Predicting ergodic sets of atomic configurations of known and hypothetical solids is a fundamental challenge in theoretical crystallography, approached via many complementary methods. We discuss this conceptually and introduce crystALL, a pragmatic and powerful data-driven approach to discovering ensembles beyond the convex hull, embracing structural diversity over stability alone, enabling robust experiment matching in cases evading traditional methods. crystALL leverages 5 million relaxed structures from OPTIMADE and MPDD to rapidly generate a comprehensive candidate set by: (1) species substitutions; (2) ML feature-space analysis with pySIPFENN; (3) representative structure selection via clustering and energy-minimization or property matching; and (4) construction of the final candidate ensemble for experimental or ab initio validation. We also uniquely address the common challenge of experimental composition uncertainties spanning many integer stoichiometries by new efficient searches across anonymized compositional ranges rather than fixed formulas. Overall, crystALL's ensemble-focused philosophy provides critical advantages over the prevailing convex-hull-searching paradigm, which often fails to account for key experimental observations and increases AI/ML discovery bias over time - akin to model collapse in LLMs retrained on iteratively generated content.</p></details> | 19 pages, 7 figures, software repository https://github.com/PhasesResearchLab/crystALL |
| **[TRUCO: A Scalable Lock-Free Algorithm for Parallel Contour Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37297v2)** | 2026-11-01 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Extracting closed pixel contours from a binary image, together with the hierarchy that records which contours enclose which, is a basic step in many computer vision pipelines, from fiducial marker detection to the vectorization of segmentation masks. The reference algorithm, by Suzuki and Abe, is inherently sequential: each contour is found by a raster scan that depends on the labels left by all previous contours, and its parent is obtained from the last contour met by the scan. This paper presents TRUCO (Threaded Raster Unrestricted Contour Ownership), a multi-core algorithm that returns exactly the output of Suzuki--Abe (the same contours, in the same order, with the same hierarchy) without locks, read-modify-write operations or a stitching phase. The method rests on two ideas. First, the image is divided into horizontal stripes, one per thread, and two rules, row-local writes and geometric contour ownership, let each thread trace complete contours independently of the others. Second, the tracing of the contours is separated from the computation of the hierarchy, which is recovered afterwards from one reference per contour; the trace therefore stores no contour numbers in the label image, which also makes it more efficient than that of the original algorithm when a single thread is used. We prove that the output is deterministic and identical to that of Suzuki--Abe, and that the algorithm is free of data races. Experiments on synthetic images, high-resolution photographs and segmentation masks, on a desktop and a server CPU, show that TRUCO scales with the number of threads and is several times faster than the fastest sequential methods, with and without the hierarchy. With a single thread, it is already faster than Suzuki--Abe.</p></details> |  |
| **[Improvise, Adapt, Overcome: An On-The-Fly Multifidelity Algorithm for Efficient Machine Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02662v2)** | 2026-10-31 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Machine learning has accelerated quantum chemistry but is hindered by the prohibitive cost of generating high fidelity training data. Multifidelity machine learning (MFML) mitigates this overhead by systematically combining abundant low fidelity data with sparse high fidelity data. In spite of its success, standard MFML schemes rely on pre-defined scaling factors to determine sparse data ratio across fidelities, often generating redundant multifidelity data resulting in a loss of efficiency. Here, we introduce an adaptive on-the-fly multifidelity framework for machine learning that autonomously determines training dataset composition. By dynamically querying training samples at each fidelity, the algorithm saturates model accuracy at lower fidelities before moving up to more expensive reference calculations. We benchmark the novel adaptive-MFML across diverse chemical properties including theoretical best estimate ab initio potential energy surfaces, golden standard coupled cluster energies, and the more chemically challenging excitation energies. In our numerical experiments we show that our adaptive algorithm reduces data generation costs by up to a factor of 20 compared to single fidelity methods and improves upon standard MFML by up to a factor of 3. The mitigation of data redundancy establishes a high-accuracy low-cost pathway for sustainable cost-aware machine learning in quantum chemistry.</p></details> | Supplementary Information added within the main text towards the end; updated several things in main text and added several things in SI |

A conservative neighbor skin preserves topology across updates, enabling each rebuild interval to replay as one CUDA Graph. Fused kernels, three-level neighbor search, and overlapped All-to-All complete it. | **[Agentic Behavior Engineering: A Design-by-Contract Methodology for Specifying, Verifying, and Governing Enterprise AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05454v1)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Enterprise teams that deploy large language model (LLM) agents inherit a maintenance problem that traditional testing does not address. ABE adapts Design by Contract to non-deterministic systems. A specification-only behavior contract with stable rule identifiers states what the agent must do, must not do, and when it must abstain for lack of evidence; a lightweight scenario library turns each rule into concrete situations; and every engineering artifact (tests, runtime policies, telemetry) references the contract in one direction only, so that specification and implementation can change independently without drifting apart. | **[Beyond the Bochner Method: Optimal Spectral Gaps for Block Dynamics of Spin Systems like the $O(N)$ Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05228v1)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Let $μ$ be a probability distribution on a spin system with $n$ sites and possibly infinite spin spaces. We prove local-to-global spectral-gap bounds for block Glauber dynamics. Given a symmetric, entrywise nonnegative matrix $A$ with zero diagonal and $λ_{\max}(A)&lt;1$ encoding local spectral bounds for a spin system, we derive global spectral-gap bounds for block dynamics on its marginal distributions. We also obtain spectral bounds for swap walks and local spectral expansion of partite simplicial complexes. For complexes formed from disjoint groups of ranks in random flags of subspaces over $\mathbb F_q$, we obtain a one-sided local spectral expansion bound of $2\sqrt q/(q+1)$. This bound is independent of the number and sizes of each group, and is nontrivial for every prime power $q$. Finally, for random forests $F$ of any finite graph with arbitrary positive edge weights, we prove that \[ \Pr(e,f\in F)\le \frac{4}{3} \Pr(e\in F)\Pr(f\in F) \] for distinct edges $e,f$, improving the factor 2 supplied by general log-concavity bounds for matroid independent sets. | **[Data-driven candidate structure ensemble generation beyond the convex hull with crystALL approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02584v1)** | 2026-11-02 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Real materials consist of multiple stable or metastable states under real-world conditions, existing as statistical ergodic ensembles of configurations rather than ideal crystals. Yet, data-driven computational materials discovery increasingly biases itself toward finding "new stable structures" from ground-state DFT calculations alone. This limits our long-term ability to discover materials whose properties emerge from metastable phases, to explain experimental observations, or to model thermodynamic behavior comprehensively. Predicting ergodic sets of atomic configurations of known and hypothetical solids is a fundamental challenge in theoretical crystallography, approached via many complementary methods. We discuss this conceptually and introduce crystALL, a pragmatic and powerful data-driven approach to discovering ensembles beyond the convex hull, embracing structural diversity over stability alone, enabling robust experiment matching in cases evading traditional methods. crystALL leverages 5 million relaxed structures from OPTIMADE and MPDD to rapidly generate a comprehensive candidate set by: (1) species substitutions; (2) ML feature-space analysis with pySIPFENN; (3) representative structure selection via clustering and energy-minimization or property matching; and (4) construction of the final candidate ensemble for experimental or ab initio validation. We also uniquely address the common challenge of experimental composition uncertainties spanning many integer stoichiometries by new efficient searches across anonymized compositional ranges rather than fixed formulas. Overall, crystALL's ensemble-focused philosophy provides critical advantages over the prevailing convex-hull-searching paradigm, which often fails to account for key experimental observations and increases AI/ML discovery bias over time - akin to model collapse in LLMs retrained on iteratively generated content.</p></details> | 19 pages, 7 figures, software repository https://github.com/PhasesResearchLab/crystALL |
| **[TRUCO: A Scalable Lock-Free Algorithm for Parallel Contour Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37297v2)** | 2026-11-01 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Extracting closed pixel contours from a binary image, together with the hierarchy that records which contours enclose which, is a basic step in many computer vision pipelines, from fiducial marker detection to the vectorization of segmentation masks. The reference algorithm, by Suzuki and Abe, is inherently sequential: each contour is found by a raster scan that depends on the labels left by all previous contours, and its parent is obtained from the last contour met by the scan. This paper presents TRUCO (Threaded Raster Unrestricted Contour Ownership), a multi-core algorithm that returns exactly the output of Suzuki--Abe (the same contours, in the same order, with the same hierarchy) without locks, read-modify-write operations or a stitching phase. The method rests on two ideas. First, the image is divided into horizontal stripes, one per thread, and two rules, row-local writes and geometric contour ownership, let each thread trace complete contours independently of the others. Second, the tracing of the contours is separated from the computation of the hierarchy, which is recovered afterwards from one reference per contour; the trace therefore stores no contour numbers in the label image, which also makes it more efficient than that of the original algorithm when a single thread is used. We prove that the output is deterministic and identical to that of Suzuki--Abe, and that the algorithm is free of data races. Experiments on synthetic images, high-resolution photographs and segmentation masks, on a desktop and a server CPU, show that TRUCO scales with the number of threads and is several times faster than the fastest sequential methods, with and without the hierarchy. With a single thread, it is already faster than Suzuki--Abe.</p></details> |  |
| **[Improvise, Adapt, Overcome: An On-The-Fly Multifidelity Algorithm for Efficient Machine Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02662v2)** | 2026-10-31 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Machine learning has accelerated quantum chemistry but is hindered by the prohibitive cost of generating high fidelity training data. Multifidelity machine learning (MFML) mitigates this overhead by systematically combining abundant low fidelity data with sparse high fidelity data. In spite of its success, standard MFML schemes rely on pre-defined scaling factors to determine sparse data ratio across fidelities, often generating redundant multifidelity data resulting in a loss of efficiency. Here, we introduce an adaptive on-the-fly multifidelity framework for machine learning that autonomously determines training dataset composition. By dynamically querying training samples at each fidelity, the algorithm saturates model accuracy at lower fidelities before moving up to more expensive reference calculations. We benchmark the novel adaptive-MFML across diverse chemical properties including theoretical best estimate ab initio potential energy surfaces, golden standard coupled cluster energies, and the more chemically challenging excitation energies. In our numerical experiments we show that our adaptive algorithm reduces data generation costs by up to a factor of 20 compared to single fidelity methods and improves upon standard MFML by up to a factor of 3. The mitigation of data redundancy establishes a high-accuracy low-cost pathway for sustainable cost-aware machine learning in quantum chemistry.</p></details> | Supplementary Information added within the main text towards the end; updated several things in main text and added several things in SI |

| **[Ground-Wing: A Parametric 3D Wing-in-Ground-Effect Dataset for Benchmarking Neural Surrogate Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04571v2)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This paper presents a CFD dataset of a highly parametrized 3D wing operating in ground effect, designed for benchmarking neural surrogate models for aerodynamic prediction, using a wing shape and flow conditions typically encountered in motorsport applications. The dataset comprises 770 RANS simulations of 194 unique wing configurations, sampled across 27 airfoil profile variants and 4 distances from the ground, spanning variations in wing sweep, chord, taper, angle of attack, and tip twist. Six neural network architectures representing distinct paradigms --- convolutional (DoMINO), graph-based (X-MeshGraphNet), transformer-based (AB-UPT, GeoTransolver, GAOT), and JEPA-based (AeroJEPA) --- are evaluated on their ability to predict surface pressure coefficient and wall shear stress distributions, from which integral drag and lift coefficients are recovered. The dataset is intended to be used as a challenging benchmark for surrogate CFD models, due to the uniqueness of the ground effect being absent from other wing and airfoil datasets. The dataset is available for download at https://huggingface.co/datasets/andreacenzato/Ground-Wing</p></details> |  |
| **[Active Learning for Gaussian Process Regression Under Self-Induced Boltzmann Weights](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.10654v2)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We consider the active learning problem where the goal is to learn an unknown function with low prediction error under an unknown Boltzmann distribution induced by the function itself. This self-induced weighting arises naturally in problems such as potential energy surface (PES) modeling in computational chemistry, yet poses unique challenges as the target distribution is unknown and its partition function is intractable. We propose \texttt{AB-SID-iVAR}, a Gaussian Process-based acquisition function that approximates the intractable Bayesian target distribution in closed form while avoiding partition function estimation, and is applicable to both discrete and continuous input domains. We also analyze a Thompson sampling alternative (\texttt{TS-SID-iVAR}) as a higher variance Monte Carlo variant. Despite the unknown target, under mild conditions, we establish that the terminal prediction error vanishes with high probability, and provide a tighter average-case guarantee. We demonstrate consistent improvements over existing approaches in this setting on synthetic benchmarks and real-world PES modeling and drug discovery tasks. Our code is available at https://github.com/TsingQAQ/SIDAL</p></details> |  |
| **[Singular Bayesian Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.00387v5)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Bayesian neural networks promise calibrated uncertainty but require $O(mn)$ parameters for standard mean-field Gaussian posteriors. We argue this cost is often unnecessary, particularly when weight matrices exhibit fast singular value decay. By parameterizing weights as $W = AB^{\top}$ with $A \in \mathbb{R}^{m \times r}$, $B \in \mathbb{R}^{n \times r}$, we induce a posterior that is \emph{singular} with respect to the Lebesgue measure, concentrating on the rank-$r$ manifold. This singularity captures structured weight correlations through shared latent factors, geometrically distinct from mean-field's independence assumption. We derive PAC-Bayes generalization bounds whose complexity term scales as $\sqrt{r(m+n)}$ instead of $\sqrt{m n}$, and prove loss bounds that decompose the error into optimization and rank-induced bias using the Eckart-Young-Mirsky theorem. We further adapt recent Gaussian complexity bounds for low-rank deterministic networks to Bayesian predictive means. Empirically, across MLPs, LSTMs, and Transformers on standard benchmarks, our method achieves competitive predictive performance while using up to $33\times$ fewer parameters than 5-member Deep Ensembles. It substantially improves OOD detection and often improves calibration relative to mean-field and perturbation baselines, while Deep Ensembles can still be stronger on in-distribution likelihood-based metrics.</p></details> | 8 pages Main text, 53 pages Appendix, 20 figures Proceedings of the 43 rd International Conference on Machine Learning (ICML 2026) |
| **[Branch-Extended Automatic Differentiation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08637v1)** | 2026-11-05 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We present branch-extended automatic differentiation (BEAD), a software package that provides information about inactive branches of piecewise-differentiable functions, intended for use in nonsmooth optimization algorithms. BEAD is implemented in Python, building on the popular JAX framework. For supported nonsmooth operations such as abs and max, BEAD identifies alternative branches within tolerance from the given input, and computes gradients of the resulting branches extended to the given input. Such information has been previously used for robust optimization methods, but has required error-prone and tedious manual differentiation, unlike BEAD, which is fully automated for functions written in NumPy-like JAX code. Our experiments show that our approach is as accurate as manually written derivative code, and offers more flexibility in modifying the function and choosing between branch-discovery rules. While we were originally motivated by the manifold sampling algorithm for nonsmooth composite objectives, we also integrate BEAD branch-extension gradients into a nonsmooth BFGS-SQP solver. On two small nonsmooth test problems, the resulting path-aware search resolves failures or substantially reduces the number of evaluations required by the BFGS-SQP solver.</p></details> |  |
| **[A Mode-Unified, Continuous, and Biomimetic Control Strategy of a Robotic Lower Limb Prosthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19183v2)** | 2026-11-04 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Existing biomimetic lower-limb prosthesis controllers are commonly designed for discrete locomotion modes, requiring separate parameterizations for level walking, ramps, and stairs. Locally optimized ascent and descent polynomial models were unified using radial basis function (RBF) interpolation, preserving the accuracy of the partitioned models while outperforming a global polynomial representation. The resulting biomechanics were transformed into continuous and slope-dependent impedance parameters and implemented within a single finite-state-machine (FSM) controller using bilateral sensing for gait-state estimation. Experiments with 10 individuals with transfemoral amputation demonstrated terrain-adaptive locomotion from level walking through ramps and stairs up to 35 degrees, including 13 degree ramp and 15 degree stair conditions absent from the underlying able-bodied (AB) dataset. Experimental results reproduced the principal slope-dependent biomechanical trends predicted by the unified model, with high agreement for knee-related measures (r-square = 0.82 - 0.99) and ankle kinematics (r-square= 0.78 - 0.96). These findings demonstrated that unified biomechanical parameterization enables a prosthesis control architecture across diverse terrain conditions while preserving key features of AB biomechanics without requiring independently tuned impedance parameter sets for each locomotion mode. To facilitate reproducibility and further development, the biomechanics modeling and impedance parameterization code is publicly available at https://github.com/kim2576.</p></details> | This work has been submitted to a research journal for possible publication. | **[Improvise, Adapt, Overcome: An On-The-Fly Multifidelity Algorithm for Efficient Machine Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02662v2)** | 2026-10-31 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Machine learning has accelerated quantum chemistry but is hindered by the prohibitive cost of generating high fidelity training data. Multifidelity machine learning (MFML) mitigates this overhead by systematically combining abundant low fidelity data with sparse high fidelity data. In spite of its success, standard MFML schemes rely on pre-defined scaling factors to determine sparse data ratio across fidelities, often generating redundant multifidelity data resulting in a loss of efficiency. Here, we introduce an adaptive on-the-fly multifidelity framework for machine learning that autonomously determines training dataset composition. By dynamically querying training samples at each fidelity, the algorithm saturates model accuracy at lower fidelities before moving up to more expensive reference calculations. We benchmark the novel adaptive-MFML across diverse chemical properties including theoretical best estimate ab initio potential energy surfaces, golden standard coupled cluster energies, and the more chemically challenging excitation energies. In our numerical experiments we show that our adaptive algorithm reduces data generation costs by up to a factor of 20 compared to single fidelity methods and improves upon standard MFML by up to a factor of 3. The mitigation of data redundancy establishes a high-accuracy low-cost pathway for sustainable cost-aware machine learning in quantum chemistry.</p></details> | Supplementary Information added within the main text towards the end; updated several things in main text and added several things in SI |

| **[Ground-Wing: A Parametric 3D Wing-in-Ground-Effect Dataset for Benchmarking Neural Surrogate Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04571v2)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This paper presents a CFD dataset of a highly parametrized 3D wing operating in ground effect, designed for benchmarking neural surrogate models for aerodynamic prediction, using a wing shape and flow conditions typically encountered in motorsport applications. The dataset comprises 770 RANS simulations of 194 unique wing configurations, sampled across 27 airfoil profile variants and 4 distances from the ground, spanning variations in wing sweep, chord, taper, angle of attack, and tip twist. Six neural network architectures representing distinct paradigms --- convolutional (DoMINO), graph-based (X-MeshGraphNet), transformer-based (AB-UPT, GeoTransolver, GAOT), and JEPA-based (AeroJEPA) --- are evaluated on their ability to predict surface pressure coefficient and wall shear stress distributions, from which integral drag and lift coefficients are recovered. The dataset is intended to be used as a challenging benchmark for surrogate CFD models, due to the uniqueness of the ground effect being absent from other wing and airfoil datasets. The dataset is available for download at https://huggingface.co/datasets/andreacenzato/Ground-Wing</p></details> |  |

We propose \texttt{AB-SID-iVAR}, a Gaussian Process-based acquisition function that approximates the intractable Bayesian target distribution in closed form while avoiding partition function estimation, and is applicable to both discrete and continuous input domains. We also analyze a Thompson sampling alternative (\texttt{TS-SID-iVAR}) as a higher variance Monte Carlo variant. Despite the unknown target, under mild conditions, we establish that the terminal prediction error vanishes with high probability, and provide a tighter average-case guarantee. | **[Improvise, Adapt, Overcome: An On-The-Fly Multifidelity Algorithm for Efficient Machine Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02662v2)** | 2026-10-31 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Machine learning has accelerated quantum chemistry but is hindered by the prohibitive cost of generating high fidelity training data. Multifidelity machine learning (MFML) mitigates this overhead by systematically combining abundant low fidelity data with sparse high fidelity data. In spite of its success, standard MFML schemes rely on pre-defined scaling factors to determine sparse data ratio across fidelities, often generating redundant multifidelity data resulting in a loss of efficiency. Here, we introduce an adaptive on-the-fly multifidelity framework for machine learning that autonomously determines training dataset composition. By dynamically querying training samples at each fidelity, the algorithm saturates model accuracy at lower fidelities before moving up to more expensive reference calculations. We benchmark the novel adaptive-MFML across diverse chemical properties including theoretical best estimate ab initio potential energy surfaces, golden standard coupled cluster energies, and the more chemically challenging excitation energies. In our numerical experiments we show that our adaptive algorithm reduces data generation costs by up to a factor of 20 compared to single fidelity methods and improves upon standard MFML by up to a factor of 3. | **[Ground-Wing: A Parametric 3D Wing-in-Ground-Effect Dataset for Benchmarking Neural Surrogate Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04571v2)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This paper presents a CFD dataset of a highly parametrized 3D wing operating in ground effect, designed for benchmarking neural surrogate models for aerodynamic prediction, using a wing shape and flow conditions typically encountered in motorsport applications. The dataset comprises 770 RANS simulations of 194 unique wing configurations, sampled across 27 airfoil profile variants and 4 distances from the ground, spanning variations in wing sweep, chord, taper, angle of attack, and tip twist.
