The dataset is available for download at https://huggingface.co/datasets/andreacenzato/Ground-Wing</p></details> |  |
| **[Active Learning for Gaussian Process Regression Under Self-Induced Boltzmann Weights](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.10654v2)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We consider the active learning problem where the goal is to learn an unknown function with low prediction error under an unknown Boltzmann distribution induced by the function itself. This self-induced weighting arises naturally in problems such as potential energy surface (PES) modeling in computational chemistry, yet poses unique challenges as the target distribution is unknown and its partition function is intractable. We propose \texttt{AB-SID-iVAR}, a Gaussian Process-based acquisition function that approximates the intractable Bayesian target distribution in closed form while avoiding partition function estimation, and is applicable to both discrete and continuous input domains. We also analyze a Thompson sampling alternative (\texttt{TS-SID-iVAR}) as a higher variance Monte Carlo variant. Despite the unknown target, under mild conditions, we establish that the terminal prediction error vanishes with high probability, and provide a tighter average-case guarantee. We demonstrate consistent improvements over existing approaches in this setting on synthetic benchmarks and real-world PES modeling and drug discovery tasks. Our code is available at https://github.com/TsingQAQ/SIDAL</p></details> |  |
| **[Singular Bayesian Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.00387v5)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Bayesian neural networks promise calibrated uncertainty but require $O(mn)$ parameters for standard mean-field Gaussian posteriors. We argue this cost is often unnecessary, particularly when weight matrices exhibit fast singular value decay. By parameterizing weights as $W = AB^{\top}$ with $A \in \mathbb{R}^{m \times r}$, $B \in \mathbb{R}^{n \times r}$, we induce a posterior that is \emph{singular} with respect to the Lebesgue measure, concentrating on the rank-$r$ manifold. This singularity captures structured weight correlations through shared latent factors, geometrically distinct from mean-field's independence assumption. We derive PAC-Bayes generalization bounds whose complexity term scales as $\sqrt{r(m+n)}$ instead of $\sqrt{m n}$, and prove loss bounds that decompose the error into optimization and rank-induced bias using the Eckart-Young-Mirsky theorem. We further adapt recent Gaussian complexity bounds for low-rank deterministic networks to Bayesian predictive means. Empirically, across MLPs, LSTMs, and Transformers on standard benchmarks, our method achieves competitive predictive performance while using up to $33\times$ fewer parameters than 5-member Deep Ensembles. It substantially improves OOD detection and often improves calibration relative to mean-field and perturbation baselines, while Deep Ensembles can still be stronger on in-distribution likelihood-based metrics.</p></details> | 8 pages Main text, 53 pages Appendix, 20 figures Proceedings of the 43 rd International Conference on Machine Learning (ICML 2026) |
| **[Branch-Extended Automatic Differentiation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08637v1)** | 2026-11-05 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We present branch-extended automatic differentiation (BEAD), a software package that provides information about inactive branches of piecewise-differentiable functions, intended for use in nonsmooth optimization algorithms. BEAD is implemented in Python, building on the popular JAX framework. For supported nonsmooth operations such as abs and max, BEAD identifies alternative branches within tolerance from the given input, and computes gradients of the resulting branches extended to the given input. Such information has been previously used for robust optimization methods, but has required error-prone and tedious manual differentiation, unlike BEAD, which is fully automated for functions written in NumPy-like JAX code. Our experiments show that our approach is as accurate as manually written derivative code, and offers more flexibility in modifying the function and choosing between branch-discovery rules. While we were originally motivated by the manifold sampling algorithm for nonsmooth composite objectives, we also integrate BEAD branch-extension gradients into a nonsmooth BFGS-SQP solver. On two small nonsmooth test problems, the resulting path-aware search resolves failures or substantially reduces the number of evaluations required by the BFGS-SQP solver.</p></details> |  |
| **[BudgetSplat: Budget-Controllable Generalizable Gaussian Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06593v1)** | 2026-11-04 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Feed-forward 3D Gaussian Splatting enables efficient generalizable novel view synthesis, but existing pixel-aligned methods often generate highly redundant Gaussian primitives, causing the final representation size and rendering cost to grow rapidly with image resolution and the number of input views. We propose BudgetSplat, a budget-controllable feed-forward framework that adapts the final Gaussian representation to a user-specified preservation ratio. Rather than treating compaction as simple importance ranking, we formulate it as a joint problem of importance estimation, spatial coverage, and cross-view redundancy. A single trained model supports varying numbers of context views and multiple test-time budgets without per-budget retraining or per-scene compression. To realize this formulation, Intra-Cross Mask Supervision provides structure-, coverage-, and redundancy-aware guidance for learning Gaussian importance, while budget-conditioned feature modulation adapts Gaussian opacity and attributes to the requested preservation ratio. Coverage-Aware Budget Selection further combines patch-wise pre-selection, view-wise opacity ranking, and 3D redundancy suppression to construct compact yet spatially distributed Gaussian representations. Experiments on indoor reconstruction and cross-dataset generalization demonstrate consistent quality--size trade-offs across different input view counts and preservation ratios, substantially reducing final representation storage and rendering cost while preserving rendering quality.</p></details> |  |
| **[A Mode-Unified, Continuous, and Biomimetic Control Strategy of a Robotic Lower Limb Prosthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19183v2)** | 2026-11-04 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Existing biomimetic lower-limb prosthesis controllers are commonly designed for discrete locomotion modes, requiring separate parameterizations for level walking, ramps, and stairs. We present a mode-unified prosthesis control framework that represents knee and ankle biomechanics continuously as functions of terrain slope and gait phase across those locomotion conditions. Locally optimized ascent and descent polynomial models were unified using radial basis function (RBF) interpolation, preserving the accuracy of the partitioned models while outperforming a global polynomial representation. The resulting biomechanics were transformed into continuous and slope-dependent impedance parameters and implemented within a single finite-state-machine (FSM) controller using bilateral sensing for gait-state estimation. Experiments with 10 individuals with transfemoral amputation demonstrated terrain-adaptive locomotion from level walking through ramps and stairs up to 35 degrees, including 13 degree ramp and 15 degree stair conditions absent from the underlying able-bodied (AB) dataset. Experimental results reproduced the principal slope-dependent biomechanical trends predicted by the unified model, with high agreement for knee-related measures (r-square = 0.82 - 0.99) and ankle kinematics (r-square= 0.78 - 0.96). These findings demonstrated that unified biomechanical parameterization enables a prosthesis control architecture across diverse terrain conditions while preserving key features of AB biomechanics without requiring independently tuned impedance parameter sets for each locomotion mode. To facilitate reproducibility and further development, the biomechanics modeling and impedance parameterization code is publicly available at https://github.com/kim2576.</p></details> | This work has been submitted to a research journal for possible publication. Copyright may be transferred without notice, after which this version may no longer be accessible |
| **[Ab Initio Accuracy Beyond the Cutoff: Distributed Message-Passing Equivariant Attention at Tens-of-Millions-of-Atom Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06122v1)** | 2026-11-04 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Molecular dynamics predicts molecular motion from atomic forces and trajectory integration. Density functional theory (DFT) provides first-principles forces, but its $O(N^3)$ cost limits simulations to thousands of atoms; empirical force fields scale to millions but miss bond breaking and charge transfer. Equivariant message-passing Transformers learn the ab initio potential at linear cost. Stacked layers propagate information beyond a single cutoff, giving four layers a receptive field up to 23Å. At this frontier, scale is limited by communication: layers exchange boundary features across devices, force backpropagation repeats the exchange, and moving atoms change neighbor graphs and tensor shapes. We present Tessera, a distributed runtime that fixes workload shapes between rebuilds. It sorts atoms along a space-filling curve into equal-count shards, ensuring identical atom counts and tensor shapes per rank. Peer sets scale as $K^{0.5}$, not $K$. A conservative neighbor skin preserves topology across updates, enabling each rebuild interval to replay as one CUDA Graph. Fused kernels, three-level neighbor search, and overlapped All-to-All complete it. On 512 GPUs, Tessera sustains 30.7M atoms at 89.7% weak-scaling efficiency (77--90% across three per-GPU loads); per-step time rises 1.11--1.29x as machine and problem grow 16x. Graph reuse cuts full-step time $1.87\times$. Across an $8\times$ device increase, neighbor-search cost grows $1.7\times$, versus $3.0\times$ for Morton order. In a live $10.24$M-atom salt solution, NVE energy drift is $0.42\,μ$eV/atom/ps over $10$ ps, and structure matches a serial reference. A 10.06M-atom Norovirus capsid sustains 100 ps of NVT at $\approx 8$ ps/day on 256 A100 GPUs. Over 200,000 steps, it has no broken bonds or anomalously close pairs and uses three compiled variants, with no graph breaks or eager fallbacks.</p></details> |  |
| **[Agentic Behavior Engineering: A Design-by-Contract Methodology for Specifying, Verifying, and Governing Enterprise AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05454v1)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Enterprise teams that deploy large language model (LLM) agents inherit a maintenance problem that traditional testing does not address. The model is non-deterministic, but the larger risk is that most of what changes an agent's behavior (a retired model version, a revised policy, an upstream schema change, a prompt edit meant to fix one case) originates outside the owning team and is discovered as a production defect rather than caught as a regression. This paper presents Agentic Behavior Engineering (ABE), a platform-agnostic methodology that treats observable agent behavior, rather than the prompt or the model, as the version-controlled engineering artifact. ABE adapts Design by Contract to non-deterministic systems. A specification-only behavior contract with stable rule identifiers states what the agent must do, must not do, and when it must abstain for lack of evidence; a lightweight scenario library turns each rule into concrete situations; and every engineering artifact (tests, runtime policies, telemetry) references the contract in one direction only, so that specification and implementation can change independently without drifting apart. The method organizes the work into five responsibilities (Specify, Ground, Control, Verify, Observe), treats missing evidence and a verified negative as different things, enforces authority outside the model, gates releases on per-scenario pass^k reliability rather than aggregate accuracy, and classifies every production run against the contract so that each finding routes to the asset that must change. We illustrate the method with an executable capacity-allocation boundary suite and a constructed worked example of a product-review approval workflow.</p></details> | 30 pages, 6 figures, 5 tables. Expanded version of the author's practitioner guide "Agentic behavior engineering" in the Microsoft Azure Architecture Center (CC BY 4.0): https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/architecture/ai-ml/guide/agentic-behavior-engineering |
| **[Beyond the Bochner Method: Optimal Spectral Gaps for Block Dynamics of Spin Systems like the $O(N)$ Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05228v1)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Let $μ$ be a probability distribution on a spin system with $n$ sites and possibly infinite spin spaces. We prove local-to-global spectral-gap bounds for block Glauber dynamics. Given a symmetric, entrywise nonnegative matrix $A$ with zero diagonal and $λ_{\max}(A)&lt;1$ encoding local spectral bounds for a spin system, we derive global spectral-gap bounds for block dynamics on its marginal distributions. These bounds are sharp: the Gaussian system with covariance $(I-A)^{-1}$ attains equality for every choice of disjoint blocks. Our proof extends the Bochner method through an abstract inequality for orthogonal projections on Hilbert spaces. As an application, we prove that Glauber dynamics for the $O(N)$ model, with $N\geq 2$, arbitrary external fields, and couplings of either sign, has spectral gap at least $(1-\&#124;J_{\mathrm{abs}}\&#124;_{\mathrm{op}}/N)/n$, where $J$ is the interaction matrix and $J_{\mathrm{abs}}=(&#124;J_{ij}&#124;)$. We also obtain spectral bounds for swap walks and local spectral expansion of partite simplicial complexes. For complexes formed from disjoint groups of ranks in random flags of subspaces over $\mathbb F_q$, we obtain a one-sided local spectral expansion bound of $2\sqrt q/(q+1)$. This bound is independent of the number and sizes of each group, and is nontrivial for every prime power $q$. Finally, for random forests $F$ of any finite graph with arbitrary positive edge weights, we prove that \[ \Pr(e,f\in F)\le \frac{4}{3} \Pr(e\in F)\Pr(f\in F) \] for distinct edges $e,f$, improving the factor 2 supplied by general log-concavity bounds for matroid independent sets. The argument combines our extended Bochner method with the analysis of an auxiliary real stable polynomial.</p></details> | 42 pages |
| **[Data-driven candidate structure ensemble generation beyond the convex hull with crystALL approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02584v1)** | 2026-11-02 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Real materials consist of multiple stable or metastable states under real-world conditions, existing as statistical ergodic ensembles of configurations rather than ideal crystals. Yet, data-driven computational materials discovery increasingly biases itself toward finding "new stable structures" from ground-state DFT calculations alone. This limits our long-term ability to discover materials whose properties emerge from metastable phases, to explain experimental observations, or to model thermodynamic behavior comprehensively. Predicting ergodic sets of atomic configurations of known and hypothetical solids is a fundamental challenge in theoretical crystallography, approached via many complementary methods. We discuss this conceptually and introduce crystALL, a pragmatic and powerful data-driven approach to discovering ensembles beyond the convex hull, embracing structural diversity over stability alone, enabling robust experiment matching in cases evading traditional methods. crystALL leverages 5 million relaxed structures from OPTIMADE and MPDD to rapidly generate a comprehensive candidate set by: (1) species substitutions; (2) ML feature-space analysis with pySIPFENN; (3) representative structure selection via clustering and energy-minimization or property matching; and (4) construction of the final candidate ensemble for experimental or ab initio validation. We also uniquely address the common challenge of experimental composition uncertainties spanning many integer stoichiometries by new efficient searches across anonymized compositional ranges rather than fixed formulas. Overall, crystALL's ensemble-focused philosophy provides critical advantages over the prevailing convex-hull-searching paradigm, which often fails to account for key experimental observations and increases AI/ML discovery bias over time - akin to model collapse in LLMs retrained on iteratively generated content.</p></details> | 19 pages, 7 figures, software repository https://github.com/PhasesResearchLab/crystALL |
| **[TRUCO: A Scalable Lock-Free Algorithm for Parallel Contour Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37297v2)** | 2026-11-01 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Extracting closed pixel contours from a binary image, together with the hierarchy that records which contours enclose which, is a basic step in many computer vision pipelines, from fiducial marker detection to the vectorization of segmentation masks. The reference algorithm, by Suzuki and Abe, is inherently sequential: each contour is found by a raster scan that depends on the labels left by all previous contours, and its parent is obtained from the last contour met by the scan. The method rests on two ideas. First, the image is divided into horizontal stripes, one per thread, and two rules, row-local writes and geometric contour ownership, let each thread trace complete contours independently of the others. Second, the tracing of the contours is separated from the computation of the hierarchy, which is recovered afterwards from one reference per contour; the trace therefore stores no contour numbers in the label image, which also makes it more efficient than that of the original algorithm when a single thread is used. We prove that the output is deterministic and identical to that of Suzuki--Abe, and that the algorithm is free of data races. Experiments on synthetic images, high-resolution photographs and segmentation masks, on a desktop and a server CPU, show that TRUCO scales with the number of threads and is several times faster than the fastest sequential methods, with and without the hierarchy. With a single thread, it is already faster than Suzuki--Abe.</p></details> |  |

This paper presents Agentic Behavior Engineering (ABE), a platform-agnostic methodology that treats observable agent behavior, rather than the prompt or the model, as the version-controlled engineering artifact. ABE adapts Design by Contract to non-deterministic systems. A specification-only behavior contract with stable rule identifiers states what the agent must do, must not do, and when it must abstain for lack of evidence; a lightweight scenario library turns each rule into concrete situations; and every engineering artifact (tests, runtime policies, telemetry) references the contract in one direction only, so that specification and implementation can change independently without drifting apart. The method organizes the work into five responsibilities (Specify, Ground, Control, Verify, Observe), treats missing evidence and a verified negative as different things, enforces authority outside the model, gates releases on per-scenario pass^k reliability rather than aggregate accuracy, and classifies every production run against the contract so that each finding routes to the asset that must change. We illustrate the method with an executable capacity-allocation boundary suite and a constructed worked example of a product-review approval workflow.</p></details> | 30 pages, 6 figures, 5 tables. Expanded version of the author's practitioner guide "Agentic behavior engineering" in the Microsoft Azure Architecture Center (CC BY 4.0): https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/architecture/ai-ml/guide/agentic-behavior-engineering |
| **[Beyond the Bochner Method: Optimal Spectral Gaps for Block Dynamics of Spin Systems like the $O(N)$ Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05228v1)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Let $μ$ be a probability distribution on a spin system with $n$ sites and possibly infinite spin spaces. We prove local-to-global spectral-gap bounds for block Glauber dynamics. Given a symmetric, entrywise nonnegative matrix $A$ with zero diagonal and $λ_{\max}(A)&lt;1$ encoding local spectral bounds for a spin system, we derive global spectral-gap bounds for block dynamics on its marginal distributions. These bounds are sharp: the Gaussian system with covariance $(I-A)^{-1}$ attains equality for every choice of disjoint blocks. Our proof extends the Bochner method through an abstract inequality for orthogonal projections on Hilbert spaces. As an application, we prove that Glauber dynamics for the $O(N)$ model, with $N\geq 2$, arbitrary external fields, and couplings of either sign, has spectral gap at least $(1-\&#124;J_{\mathrm{abs}}\&#124;_{\mathrm{op}}/N)/n$, where $J$ is the interaction matrix and $J_{\mathrm{abs}}=(&#124;J_{ij}&#124;)$. We also obtain spectral bounds for swap walks and local spectral expansion of partite simplicial complexes. For complexes formed from disjoint groups of ranks in random flags of subspaces over $\mathbb F_q$, we obtain a one-sided local spectral expansion bound of $2\sqrt q/(q+1)$. This bound is independent of the number and sizes of each group, and is nontrivial for every prime power $q$. Finally, for random forests $F$ of any finite graph with arbitrary positive edge weights, we prove that \[ \Pr(e,f\in F)\le \frac{4}{3} \Pr(e\in F)\Pr(f\in F) \] for distinct edges $e,f$, improving the factor 2 supplied by general log-concavity bounds for matroid independent sets. The argument combines our extended Bochner method with the analysis of an auxiliary real stable polynomial.</p></details> | 42 pages |
| **[Data-driven candidate structure ensemble generation beyond the convex hull with crystALL approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02584v1)** | 2026-11-02 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Real materials consist of multiple stable or metastable states under real-world conditions, existing as statistical ergodic ensembles of configurations rather than ideal crystals. Yet, data-driven computational materials discovery increasingly biases itself toward finding "new stable structures" from ground-state DFT calculations alone. This limits our long-term ability to discover materials whose properties emerge from metastable phases, to explain experimental observations, or to model thermodynamic behavior comprehensively. Predicting ergodic sets of atomic configurations of known and hypothetical solids is a fundamental challenge in theoretical crystallography, approached via many complementary methods. We discuss this conceptually and introduce crystALL, a pragmatic and powerful data-driven approach to discovering ensembles beyond the convex hull, embracing structural diversity over stability alone, enabling robust experiment matching in cases evading traditional methods. crystALL leverages 5 million relaxed structures from OPTIMADE and MPDD to rapidly generate a comprehensive candidate set by: (1) species substitutions; (2) ML feature-space analysis with pySIPFENN; (3) representative structure selection via clustering and energy-minimization or property matching; and (4) construction of the final candidate ensemble for experimental or ab initio validation. We also uniquely address the common challenge of experimental composition uncertainties spanning many integer stoichiometries by new efficient searches across anonymized compositional ranges rather than fixed formulas. Overall, crystALL's ensemble-focused philosophy provides critical advantages over the prevailing convex-hull-searching paradigm, which often fails to account for key experimental observations and increases AI/ML discovery bias over time - akin to model collapse in LLMs retrained on iteratively generated content.</p></details> | 19 pages, 7 figures, software repository https://github.com/PhasesResearchLab/crystALL |
| **[TRUCO: A Scalable Lock-Free Algorithm for Parallel Contour Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37297v2)** | 2026-11-01 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Extracting closed pixel contours from a binary image, together with the hierarchy that records which contours enclose which, is a basic step in many computer vision pipelines, from fiducial marker detection to the vectorization of segmentation masks. The reference algorithm, by Suzuki and Abe, is inherently sequential: each contour is found by a raster scan that depends on the labels left by all previous contours, and its parent is obtained from the last contour met by the scan. This paper presents TRUCO (Threaded Raster Unrestricted Contour Ownership), a multi-core algorithm that returns exactly the output of Suzuki--Abe (the same contours, in the same order, with the same hierarchy) without locks, read-modify-write operations or a stitching phase. The method rests on two ideas. First, the image is divided into horizontal stripes, one per thread, and two rules, row-local writes and geometric contour ownership, let each thread trace complete contours independently of the others. Second, the tracing of the contours is separated from the computation of the hierarchy, which is recovered afterwards from one reference per contour; the trace therefore stores no contour numbers in the label image, which also makes it more efficient than that of the original algorithm when a single thread is used. We prove that the output is deterministic and identical to that of Suzuki--Abe, and that the algorithm is free of data races. Experiments on synthetic images, high-resolution photographs and segmentation masks, on a desktop and a server CPU, show that TRUCO scales with the number of threads and is several times faster than the fastest sequential methods, with and without the hierarchy. With a single thread, it is already faster than Suzuki--Abe.</p></details> |  |
| **[Improvise, Adapt, Overcome: An On-The-Fly Multifidelity Algorithm for Efficient Machine Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02662v2)** | 2026-10-31 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Machine learning has accelerated quantum chemistry but is hindered by the prohibitive cost of generating high fidelity training data. Multifidelity machine learning (MFML) mitigates this overhead by systematically combining abundant low fidelity data with sparse high fidelity data. In spite of its success, standard MFML schemes rely on pre-defined scaling factors to determine sparse data ratio across fidelities, often generating redundant multifidelity data resulting in a loss of efficiency. Here, we introduce an adaptive on-the-fly multifidelity framework for machine learning that autonomously determines training dataset composition. By dynamically querying training samples at each fidelity, the algorithm saturates model accuracy at lower fidelities before moving up to more expensive reference calculations. We benchmark the novel adaptive-MFML across diverse chemical properties including theoretical best estimate ab initio potential energy surfaces, golden standard coupled cluster energies, and the more chemically challenging excitation energies. In our numerical experiments we show that our adaptive algorithm reduces data generation costs by up to a factor of 20 compared to single fidelity methods and improves upon standard MFML by up to a factor of 3. The mitigation of data redundancy establishes a high-accuracy low-cost pathway for sustainable cost-aware machine learning in quantum chemistry.</p></details> | Supplementary Information added within the main text towards the end; updated several things in main text and added several things in SI |
| **[Non-Gaussian Uncertainty Estimation via Semi-nonparametric Density](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.09375v2)** | 2026-10-30 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Accurate and efficient representation of non-Gaussian state distributions is critical for estimation, control, and decision-making, especially when the system is highly nonlinear. This paper develops a mathematical framework for estimating non-Gaussian uncertainty via semi-nonparametric (SNP), or Gallant-Nychka, densities. SNP densities use squared probabilists' Hermite polynomials to guarantee nonnegative probability densities while representing non-Gaussian behavior. For uncertainty propagation, once fitted to propagated state samples, SNP densities enable direct analytical density and probability evaluations without needing to tune additional hyperparameters besides the polynomial order. To enable their application to nonlinear dynamical systems, we derive analytic marginal and cumulative distribution functions and develop a lossless convex formulation of the coefficient estimation problem along a given sign branch. The approach is demonstrated on the chaotic Lorenz system and compared against Gaussian mixture models and Edgeworth approximations. Results demonstrate that SNP densities can accurately represent non-Gaussian state distributions while enabling direct probability and quantile evaluation, making them a promising tool for density estimation and uncertainty quantification in nonlinear estimation and control.</p></details> |  |

## feed-forward 3D reconstruction
| **Title** | **Date** | **Abstract** | **Comment** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **[Click3R: Interactive Stereo 3D Reconstruction with Sparse Correspondence Clicks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12875v1)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Despite impressive progress, existing feed-forward large 3D reconstruction models produce a single, irrevocable prediction. When these models fail, there is no mechanism to correct their mistakes. To address this limitation, we introduce Click3R, a framework for interactive geometric correction of feed-forward models using sparse human correspondence clicks. Given an image pair and a small set of cross-view point correspondences provided by a user, Click3R injects geometric constraints into the 3D reconstruction networks through a lightweight point correspondence adapter, enabling the model to resolve ambiguities in challenging scenarios such as repetitive textures, symmetric structures, large viewpoint changes, or textureless regions. To evaluate interactive reconstruction, we curate a challenging benchmark specifically designed to expose failure modes of existing feed-forward 3D reconstruction methods. Experiments show that Click3R reduces reconstruction error with as few as a single correspondence click, improving performance on both standard and our curated benchmarks. Our results highlight that sparse human interaction provides an effective and practical mechanism for correcting geometric reconstruction errors.</p></details> | NeurIPS 2026. Project page: https://uva-computer-vision-lab.github.io/click3r/ |
| **[Ground-Wing: A Parametric 3D Wing-in-Ground-Effect Dataset for Benchmarking Neural Surrogate Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04571v2)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This paper presents a CFD dataset of a highly parametrized 3D wing operating in ground effect, designed for benchmarking neural surrogate models for aerodynamic prediction, using a wing shape and flow conditions typically encountered in motorsport applications. The dataset comprises 770 RANS simulations of 194 unique wing configurations, sampled across 27 airfoil profile variants and 4 distances from the ground, spanning variations in wing sweep, chord, taper, angle of attack, and tip twist. Six neural network architectures representing distinct paradigms --- convolutional (DoMINO), graph-based (X-MeshGraphNet), transformer-based (AB-UPT, GeoTransolver, GAOT), and JEPA-based (AeroJEPA) --- are evaluated on their ability to predict surface pressure coefficient and wall shear stress distributions, from which integral drag and lift coefficients are recovered. The dataset is intended to be used as a challenging benchmark for surrogate CFD models, due to the uniqueness of the ground effect being absent from other wing and airfoil datasets. The dataset is available for download at https://huggingface.co/datasets/andreacenzato/Ground-Wing</p></details> |  |
| **[3R-Adapter: Retrieval, Rewiring, and Refinement for Efficient Adaptation of 3D Reconstruction Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11964v1)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Feed-forward 3D reconstruction models have demonstrated strong generalization under large-scale pre-training, yet they struggle in long-tail scenarios such as low-light reconstruction, joint human-scene reconstruction, and test-time adaptation. We observe relational attention structure collapse, manifested as the failure of cross-view correspondences, as a primary cause of these limitations. While adaptation offers a practical way to bridge this gap, existing parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) approaches mainly perform channel-wise feature adjustment and lack mechanisms to explicitly adapt relational structures, leaving correspondence failures unresolved. In this work, we formulate adaptation of 3D reconstruction models as a problem of correspondence recovery under domain shift and propose 3R-Adapter, a PEFT paradigm for long-tail 3D reconstruction scenarios. Our method consists of three components: Deformable Retrieval for task-adaptive relation candidate retrieval, Relational Rewiring for reconstructing cross-view relational structures, and Consistency Refinement for injecting the rewired relations into stable predictions. Extensive experiments on low-light reconstruction, joint human-scene reconstruction, and test-time adaptation demonstrate that our approach significantly improves robustness over existing PEFT methods while maintaining efficient lightweight adaptation. Our code is released at https://github.com/PKU-OV3-LAB/3R-Adapter.</p></details> | Accepted as NeurIPS 2026 Spotlight |
| **[Active Learning for Gaussian Process Regression Under Self-Induced Boltzmann Weights](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.10654v2)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We consider the active learning problem where the goal is to learn an unknown function with low prediction error under an unknown Boltzmann distribution induced by the function itself. This self-induced weighting arises naturally in problems such as potential energy surface (PES) modeling in computational chemistry, yet poses unique challenges as the target distribution is unknown and its partition function is intractable. We propose \texttt{AB-SID-iVAR}, a Gaussian Process-based acquisition function that approximates the intractable Bayesian target distribution in closed form while avoiding partition function estimation, and is applicable to both discrete and continuous input domains. We also analyze a Thompson sampling alternative (\texttt{TS-SID-iVAR}) as a higher variance Monte Carlo variant. Despite the unknown target, under mild conditions, we establish that the terminal prediction error vanishes with high probability, and provide a tighter average-case guarantee. We demonstrate consistent improvements over existing approaches in this setting on synthetic benchmarks and real-world PES modeling and drug discovery tasks. Our code is available at https://github.com/TsingQAQ/SIDAL</p></details> |  |
| **[Singular Bayesian Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.00387v5)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Bayesian neural networks promise calibrated uncertainty but require $O(mn)$ parameters for standard mean-field Gaussian posteriors. We argue this cost is often unnecessary, particularly when weight matrices exhibit fast singular value decay. By parameterizing weights as $W = AB^{\top}$ with $A \in \mathbb{R}^{m \times r}$, $B \in \mathbb{R}^{n \times r}$, we induce a posterior that is \emph{singular} with respect to the Lebesgue measure, concentrating on the rank-$r$ manifold. This singularity captures structured weight correlations through shared latent factors, geometrically distinct from mean-field's independence assumption. We derive PAC-Bayes generalization bounds whose complexity term scales as $\sqrt{r(m+n)}$ instead of $\sqrt{m n}$, and prove loss bounds that decompose the error into optimization and rank-induced bias using the Eckart-Young-Mirsky theorem. We further adapt recent Gaussian complexity bounds for low-rank deterministic networks to Bayesian predictive means. Empirically, across MLPs, LSTMs, and Transformers on standard benchmarks, our method achieves competitive predictive performance while using up to $33\times$ fewer parameters than 5-member Deep Ensembles. It substantially improves OOD detection and often improves calibration relative to mean-field and perturbation baselines, while Deep Ensembles can still be stronger on in-distribution likelihood-based metrics.</p></details> | 8 pages Main text, 53 pages Appendix, 20 figures Proceedings of the 43 rd International Conference on Machine Learning (ICML 2026) |
| **[OfficeTwin: Realizing 3D Reconstruction of Redundant Scenes through Partial Viewpoint Perception](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10241v1)** | 2026-11-06 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Feed-forward 3D reconstruction turns an uncalibrated monocular stream into a dense point cloud in a single inference pass, orders of magnitude faster than classical structure-from-motion (e.g., COLMAP), and reliable on the weakly textured, repetitive surfaces where keypoint matching fails outright. However, raw point clouds lack the object identity, physical properties, and watertight volume required for robot learning. This gap widens under robot-native capture. A camera mounted on a robot constrained to a few degrees of freedom sees each object only partially, and the repetitive, weakly textured structure of an office makes distinct workstations nearly indistinguishable, leaving redundant and partial observations that downstream reconstruction cannot resolve. Existing image-to-3D generation methods struggle with these conditions, hallucinating geometry under incomplete views and confusing repeated instances. We present OfficeTwin, a robot-native framework that converts these incomplete observations into simulation-ready digital twins. OfficeTwin first imposes metric and structural constraints, then exploits the office's structural redundancy by registering repeated cubicles into a shared canonical frame, where complementary observations recover geometry occluded in any single view without hallucination. On a real office scan, OfficeTwin recovers every cubicle as an individually watertight, metric solid, outperforming feed-forward reconstruction and generative baselines that fail to produce simulation-ready assets.</p></details> |  |
| **[A Straight Line Is Not a Camera Pose: Auditing Short-Clip Pose Benchmarks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00482v2)** | 2026-11-06 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>The absolute trajectory error (ATE) is the standard measure of camera-motion accuracy, from SLAM to feed-forward 3D reconstruction. On the common Sintel protocol, an image-free constant-velocity line has a lower mean ATE than 10 of 11 recent models re-run with official code and than 85 of 110 published methods. On Lightspeed it beats 5 of 9 models and 10 of 11 published methods, while on TUM, ScanNet, DyCheck and ADT every model beats it. Two properties of ATE's alignment explain this. First, it maps any moving constant-velocity prediction onto the line that best fits the true path, so the line scores the path's regression residual. A residual signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) criterion tells when moving a prediction towards its constant-velocity fit lowers ATE. With the alignment's first-order refit, it accounts for the outcome of all but one of 4010 model-clip evaluations, including all 504 on three benchmarks registered before any model ran. The true path's straightness flags Sintel and Lightspeed. Second, the alignment ignores where cameras look: rotating a trajectory by 30 degrees against its orientations leaves ATE unchanged yet worsens the static-point reprojection of every model on Sintel, TUM and ScanNet, and the line reprojects worst on Sintel by geometric mean. Fitting the alignment's rotation to the predicted orientations gives pose-anchored ATE (PA-ATE), a one-function change at about ATE's cost (37 vs. 34 $μ$s per clip, and 61 vs. 66 $μ$s with the rotation error). It is never below ATE and, as we prove, equals the translational relative pose error over all frame pairs divided by $\sqrt{2}$ when the orientations agree. It ranks every image-free reference below every model on Sintel, follows the reprojection error where ATE does not, and largely preserves the models' order. Its rotation error also exposes Lightspeed ground-truth orientations that the images contradict.</p></details> | 39 pages, 9 figures |
| **[Branch-Extended Automatic Differentiation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08637v1)** | 2026-11-05 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We present branch-extended automatic differentiation (BEAD), a software package that provides information about inactive branches of piecewise-differentiable functions, intended for use in nonsmooth optimization algorithms. BEAD is implemented in Python, building on the popular JAX framework. For supported nonsmooth operations such as abs and max, BEAD identifies alternative branches within tolerance from the given input, and computes gradients of the resulting branches extended to the given input. Such information has been previously used for robust optimization methods, but has required error-prone and tedious manual differentiation, unlike BEAD, which is fully automated for functions written in NumPy-like JAX code. Our experiments show that our approach is as accurate as manually written derivative code, and offers more flexibility in modifying the function and choosing between branch-discovery rules. While we were originally motivated by the manifold sampling algorithm for nonsmooth composite objectives, we also integrate BEAD branch-extension gradients into a nonsmooth BFGS-SQP solver. On two small nonsmooth test problems, the resulting path-aware search resolves failures or substantially reduces the number of evaluations required by the BFGS-SQP solver.</p></details> |  |
| **[Rotation Is Token-Blind: A Structural Criterion for Post-Training Quantization of Feed-Forward Geometry Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07955v1)** | 2026-11-05 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Feed-forward 3D models use Transformers to predict camera poses, depth, and structure in one pass, avoiding iterative multi-view optimization. Long sequences and deep attention incur high compute and memory costs, motivating Post-Training Quantization (PTQ) for deployment. Existing PTQ methods mainly suppress channel-wise outliers by smoothing or orthogonal rotation. Geometry Transformers introduce a different challenge: heterogeneous token types share one activation quantization grid. A few special tokens may exert high leverage over global outputs and be updated by only a subset of blocks. Token-blind transforms, which apply the same transformation to every token, reduce overall quantization error but cannot remove differentiated degradation across token types. We propose QMapAnything, a structure-aware PTQ framework. We cast floating-point-preserving rotation and smoothing as a token-blind reparameterization group, and prove that they yield common-mode gains while leaving relative token SNR unchanged. We introduce a statistic $Γ$ that combines a special token's quantization disadvantage and output leverage to predict whether differentiated bias correction is needed beyond rotation. Experiments on geometric benchmarks show that W4A8 closely matches full-precision performance. Under W8A8, peak memory falls by $2.2$--$3.1\times$ versus FP32, with up to $3.1\times$ speedup on real hardware. These results provide a principled and efficient approach to deploying feed-forward 3D reconstruction models in resource-constrained edges.</p></details> |  |
| **[A Mode-Unified, Continuous, and Biomimetic Control Strategy of a Robotic Lower Limb Prosthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19183v2)** | 2026-11-04 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Existing biomimetic lower-limb prosthesis controllers are commonly designed for discrete locomotion modes, requiring separate parameterizations for level walking, ramps, and stairs. We present a mode-unified prosthesis control framework that represents knee and ankle biomechanics continuously as functions of terrain slope and gait phase across those locomotion conditions. Locally optimized ascent and descent polynomial models were unified using radial basis function (RBF) interpolation, preserving the accuracy of the partitioned models while outperforming a global polynomial representation. The resulting biomechanics were transformed into continuous and slope-dependent impedance parameters and implemented within a single finite-state-machine (FSM) controller using bilateral sensing for gait-state estimation. Experiments with 10 individuals with transfemoral amputation demonstrated terrain-adaptive locomotion from level walking through ramps and stairs up to 35 degrees, including 13 degree ramp and 15 degree stair conditions absent from the underlying able-bodied (AB) dataset. Experimental results reproduced the principal slope-dependent biomechanical trends predicted by the unified model, with high agreement for knee-related measures (r-square = 0.82 - 0.99) and ankle kinematics (r-square= 0.78 - 0.96). These findings demonstrated that unified biomechanical parameterization enables a prosthesis control architecture across diverse terrain conditions while preserving key features of AB biomechanics without requiring independently tuned impedance parameter sets for each locomotion mode. To facilitate reproducibility and further development, the biomechanics modeling and impedance parameterization code is publicly available at https://github.com/kim2576.</p></details> | This work has been submitted to a research journal for possible publication. Copyright may be transferred without notice, after which this version may no longer be accessible |
| **[Ab Initio Accuracy Beyond the Cutoff: Distributed Message-Passing Equivariant Attention at Tens-of-Millions-of-Atom Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06122v1)** | 2026-11-04 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Molecular dynamics predicts molecular motion from atomic forces and trajectory integration. Density functional theory (DFT) provides first-principles forces, but its $O(N^3)$ cost limits simulations to thousands of atoms; empirical force fields scale to millions but miss bond breaking and charge transfer. Equivariant message-passing Transformers learn the ab initio potential at linear cost. Stacked layers propagate information beyond a single cutoff, giving four layers a receptive field up to 23Å. At this frontier, scale is limited by communication: layers exchange boundary features across devices, force backpropagation repeats the exchange, and moving atoms change neighbor graphs and tensor shapes. We present Tessera, a distributed runtime that fixes workload shapes between rebuilds. It sorts atoms along a space-filling curve into equal-count shards, ensuring identical atom counts and tensor shapes per rank. Peer sets scale as $K^{0.5}$, not $K$. A conservative neighbor skin preserves topology across updates, enabling each rebuild interval to replay as one CUDA Graph. Fused kernels, three-level neighbor search, and overlapped All-to-All complete it. On 512 GPUs, Tessera sustains 30.7M atoms at 89.7% weak-scaling efficiency (77--90% across three per-GPU loads); per-step time rises 1.11--1.29x as machine and problem grow 16x. Graph reuse cuts full-step time $1.87\times$. Across an $8\times$ device increase, neighbor-search cost grows $1.7\times$, versus $3.0\times$ for Morton order. In a live $10.24$M-atom salt solution, NVE energy drift is $0.42\,μ$eV/atom/ps over $10$ ps, and structure matches a serial reference. A 10.06M-atom Norovirus capsid sustains 100 ps of NVT at $\approx 8$ ps/day on 256 A100 GPUs. Over 200,000 steps, it has no broken bonds or anomalously close pairs and uses three compiled variants, with no graph breaks or eager fallbacks.</p></details> |  |
| **[Feature Puzzles: Single-Image Supervision for Multi-View 3D-Aware Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05874v1)** | 2026-11-04 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Humans can easily recognize the same objects across different viewpoints of a 3D scene, whereas teaching models such multi-view-consistent perception typically requires videos or calibrated multi-view captures, which are considerably more expensive and less diverse than large-scale monocular data.~In this paper, we introduce Feature Puzzles, a novel framework for learning multi-view-consistent segmentation using only single-image semantic segmentation supervision. Specifically, we propose a simple yet effective pipeline that constructs multi-view-consistent segmentation pseudo-labels through image cropping and geometric transformations. We further introduce a lightweight, plug-and-play network that leverages the multi-view-consistent image features learned by existing pretrained 3D foundation models and the generated pseudo-labels to perform multi-view-consistent segmentation. Importantly, our flexible framework supports various types of prompts provided from one or multiple views and can be readily extended to open-vocabulary 3D segmentation. Under this single-image supervision, our feed-forward model achieves the best mIoU on NVOS, remains competitive on SPIn-NeRF and ScanNet++ against baselines using per-scene optimization or in-domain multi-view training, and demonstrates zero-shot transfer to open-vocabulary 3D segmentation. Visual comparisons are available on our project page: https://featurepuzzles.github.io/</p></details> | Feed Forward 3D Reconstruction and Spatial Understanding, Multi-view Promptable Segmentation, Instance Tracking |

| **[ReVar3R: Gauge-Aware Perturbation Uncertainty for Feed-Forward 3D Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.07883v2)** | 2026-11-02 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>A correctly reconstructed distant point appears uncertain even when a frozen 3D model processes equivalent inputs because its output frame rotates fractionally. This exposes a weakness of trainingfree perturbation uncertainty: when outputs contain an unobserved symmetry, run-to-run variation potentially reflects symmetry rather than error. Existing alternatives have trade-offs: built-in confidence is outperformed in most evaluated conditions, while trained evidential heads require modelspecific supervision. For point maps, this research derives a closed-form, error-independent variance term that grows with scene extent and potentially overwhelms the desired signal. Simulation reproduces the effect; all 30 real VGGT view-sets tested exhibit its predicted $\&#124;x_p\&#124;^2$ signature. ReVar3R robustly registers predictions to a common similarity frame before computing per-point variance, without retraining or modifying the frozen model. Optional calibration and fusion use a held-out split. Across VGGT, π3, and MASt3R on six datasets, the same estimator on every backbone lowers AUSE below built-in confidence in 15 of 18 conditions. The staged evaluation yields 11 of 18 wins for the label-free core, 12/18 for label-free equal-weight fusion, 14/18 with held-out weights, and 15/18 when the built-in signal is included. Against a trained evidential head, the result is a trade-off: the head calibrates magnitude better and leads in its training domain, whereas ReVar3R transfers across backbones without adaptation. | **[Data-driven candidate structure ensemble generation beyond the convex hull with crystALL approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02584v1)** | 2026-11-02 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Real materials consist of multiple stable or metastable states under real-world conditions, existing as statistical ergodic ensembles of configurations rather than ideal crystals. Yet, data-driven computational materials discovery increasingly biases itself toward finding "new stable structures" from ground-state DFT calculations alone. This limits our long-term ability to discover materials whose properties emerge from metastable phases, to explain experimental observations, or to model thermodynamic behavior comprehensively. Predicting ergodic sets of atomic configurations of known and hypothetical solids is a fundamental challenge in theoretical crystallography, approached via many complementary methods. We discuss this conceptually and introduce crystALL, a pragmatic and powerful data-driven approach to discovering ensembles beyond the convex hull, embracing structural diversity over stability alone, enabling robust experiment matching in cases evading traditional methods. crystALL leverages 5 million relaxed structures from OPTIMADE and MPDD to rapidly generate a comprehensive candidate set by: (1) species substitutions; (2) ML feature-space analysis with pySIPFENN; (3) representative structure selection via clustering and energy-minimization or property matching; and (4) construction of the final candidate ensemble for experimental or ab initio validation. We also uniquely address the common challenge of experimental composition uncertainties spanning many integer stoichiometries by new efficient searches across anonymized compositional ranges rather than fixed formulas. Overall, crystALL's ensemble-focused philosophy provides critical advantages over the prevailing convex-hull-searching paradigm, which often fails to account for key experimental observations and increases AI/ML discovery bias over time - akin to model collapse in LLMs retrained on iteratively generated content.</p></details> | 19 pages, 7 figures, software repository https://github.com/PhasesResearchLab/crystALL |
| **[TRUCO: A Scalable Lock-Free Algorithm for Parallel Contour Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37297v2)** | 2026-11-01 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Extracting closed pixel contours from a binary image, together with the hierarchy that records which contours enclose which, is a basic step in many computer vision pipelines, from fiducial marker detection to the vectorization of segmentation masks. The method rests on two ideas. First, the image is divided into horizontal stripes, one per thread, and two rules, row-local writes and geometric contour ownership, let each thread trace complete contours independently of the others. Second, the tracing of the contours is separated from the computation of the hierarchy, which is recovered afterwards from one reference per contour; the trace therefore stores no contour numbers in the label image, which also makes it more efficient than that of the original algorithm when a single thread is used. We prove that the output is deterministic and identical to that of Suzuki--Abe, and that the algorithm is free of data races. Experiments on synthetic images, high-resolution photographs and segmentation masks, on a desktop and a server CPU, show that TRUCO scales with the number of threads and is several times faster than the fastest sequential methods, with and without the hierarchy. | **[Improvise, Adapt, Overcome: An On-The-Fly Multifidelity Algorithm for Efficient Machine Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02662v2)** | 2026-10-31 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Machine learning has accelerated quantum chemistry but is hindered by the prohibitive cost of generating high fidelity training data. Multifidelity machine learning (MFML) mitigates this overhead by systematically combining abundant low fidelity data with sparse high fidelity data. In spite of its success, standard MFML schemes rely on pre-defined scaling factors to determine sparse data ratio across fidelities, often generating redundant multifidelity data resulting in a loss of efficiency. Here, we introduce an adaptive on-the-fly multifidelity framework for machine learning that autonomously determines training dataset composition. By dynamically querying training samples at each fidelity, the algorithm saturates model accuracy at lower fidelities before moving up to more expensive reference calculations. We benchmark the novel adaptive-MFML across diverse chemical properties including theoretical best estimate ab initio potential energy surfaces, golden standard coupled cluster energies, and the more chemically challenging excitation energies. In our numerical experiments we show that our adaptive algorithm reduces data generation costs by up to a factor of 20 compared to single fidelity methods and improves upon standard MFML by up to a factor of 3. The mitigation of data redundancy establishes a high-accuracy low-cost pathway for sustainable cost-aware machine learning in quantum chemistry.</p></details> | Supplementary Information added within the main text towards the end; updated several things in main text and added several things in SI |

## feed-forward Gaussian Splatting
| **Title** | **Date** | **Abstract** | **Comment** |

| **[Ground-Wing: A Parametric 3D Wing-in-Ground-Effect Dataset for Benchmarking Neural Surrogate Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04571v2)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This paper presents a CFD dataset of a highly parametrized 3D wing operating in ground effect, designed for benchmarking neural surrogate models for aerodynamic prediction, using a wing shape and flow conditions typically encountered in motorsport applications. The dataset comprises 770 RANS simulations of 194 unique wing configurations, sampled across 27 airfoil profile variants and 4 distances from the ground, spanning variations in wing sweep, chord, taper, angle of attack, and tip twist. Six neural network architectures representing distinct paradigms --- convolutional (DoMINO), graph-based (X-MeshGraphNet), transformer-based (AB-UPT, GeoTransolver, GAOT), and JEPA-based (AeroJEPA) --- are evaluated on their ability to predict surface pressure coefficient and wall shear stress distributions, from which integral drag and lift coefficients are recovered. The dataset is intended to be used as a challenging benchmark for surrogate CFD models, due to the uniqueness of the ground effect being absent from other wing and airfoil datasets. The dataset is available for download at https://huggingface.co/datasets/andreacenzato/Ground-Wing</p></details> |  |
| **[UltraVoxelGS: Voxel-First Feed-Forward Gaussian Splatting for 3D Ultrasound Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11951v1)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We present UltraVoxelGS, a 3D ultrasound reconstruction method that achieves both fast reconstruction and physically faithful rendering for the first time, bringing live spatial feedback during 2D ultrasound scanning within reach. This is made possible by a feed-forward design that predicts a complete Gaussian scene representation from posed ultrasound images directly, entirely bypassing the per-scene optimization bottleneck that has confined prior methods to offline use. Realising such feed-forward prediction in this setting is non-trivial: unlike natural images, ultrasound slices share at most one-dimensional intersections and exhibit view-dependent appearance due to acoustic propagation and attenuation, violating the dense overlap and photometric consistency assumed by existing feed-forward Gaussian methods. Hence, to achieve fast reconstruction, we introduce a feed-forward voxel-to-Gaussian pass that decouples representation size from input count by lifting all posed slices into a fixed-size voxel support and predicting Gaussian parameters directly. For faithful rendering, we propose ultrasound-aware appearance rendering strategy factorize reflection from cumulative attenuation during training and exploit appearance continuity among spatially adjacent slices for efficient Render-time Appearance Adaptation. On both real-world and simulated datasets, UltraVoxelGS achieves best PSNR/SSIM compared with per-scene optimization baselines while reducing inference from 10-20 minutes to approximately 10 seconds (over 35 times faster) and scales to sequences exceeding 1,000 frames. Code is available at https://github.com/YuebyYue/UltraVoxelGS-camera-ready.</p></details> |  |
| **[Active Learning for Gaussian Process Regression Under Self-Induced Boltzmann Weights](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.10654v2)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We consider the active learning problem where the goal is to learn an unknown function with low prediction error under an unknown Boltzmann distribution induced by the function itself. This self-induced weighting arises naturally in problems such as potential energy surface (PES) modeling in computational chemistry, yet poses unique challenges as the target distribution is unknown and its partition function is intractable. We propose \texttt{AB-SID-iVAR}, a Gaussian Process-based acquisition function that approximates the intractable Bayesian target distribution in closed form while avoiding partition function estimation, and is applicable to both discrete and continuous input domains. We also analyze a Thompson sampling alternative (\texttt{TS-SID-iVAR}) as a higher variance Monte Carlo variant. Despite the unknown target, under mild conditions, we establish that the terminal prediction error vanishes with high probability, and provide a tighter average-case guarantee. We demonstrate consistent improvements over existing approaches in this setting on synthetic benchmarks and real-world PES modeling and drug discovery tasks. Our code is available at https://github.com/TsingQAQ/SIDAL</p></details> |  |
| **[Singular Bayesian Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.00387v5)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Bayesian neural networks promise calibrated uncertainty but require $O(mn)$ parameters for standard mean-field Gaussian posteriors. We argue this cost is often unnecessary, particularly when weight matrices exhibit fast singular value decay. By parameterizing weights as $W = AB^{\top}$ with $A \in \mathbb{R}^{m \times r}$, $B \in \mathbb{R}^{n \times r}$, we induce a posterior that is \emph{singular} with respect to the Lebesgue measure, concentrating on the rank-$r$ manifold. This singularity captures structured weight correlations through shared latent factors, geometrically distinct from mean-field's independence assumption. We derive PAC-Bayes generalization bounds whose complexity term scales as $\sqrt{r(m+n)}$ instead of $\sqrt{m n}$, and prove loss bounds that decompose the error into optimization and rank-induced bias using the Eckart-Young-Mirsky theorem. We further adapt recent Gaussian complexity bounds for low-rank deterministic networks to Bayesian predictive means. Empirically, across MLPs, LSTMs, and Transformers on standard benchmarks, our method achieves competitive predictive performance while using up to $33\times$ fewer parameters than 5-member Deep Ensembles. It substantially improves OOD detection and often improves calibration relative to mean-field and perturbation baselines, while Deep Ensembles can still be stronger on in-distribution likelihood-based metrics.</p></details> | 8 pages Main text, 53 pages Appendix, 20 figures Proceedings of the 43 rd International Conference on Machine Learning (ICML 2026) |
| **[Branch-Extended Automatic Differentiation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08637v1)** | 2026-11-05 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We present branch-extended automatic differentiation (BEAD), a software package that provides information about inactive branches of piecewise-differentiable functions, intended for use in nonsmooth optimization algorithms. BEAD is implemented in Python, building on the popular JAX framework. For supported nonsmooth operations such as abs and max, BEAD identifies alternative branches within tolerance from the given input, and computes gradients of the resulting branches extended to the given input. Such information has been previously used for robust optimization methods, but has required error-prone and tedious manual differentiation, unlike BEAD, which is fully automated for functions written in NumPy-like JAX code. Our experiments show that our approach is as accurate as manually written derivative code, and offers more flexibility in modifying the function and choosing between branch-discovery rules. While we were originally motivated by the manifold sampling algorithm for nonsmooth composite objectives, we also integrate BEAD branch-extension gradients into a nonsmooth BFGS-SQP solver. On two small nonsmooth test problems, the resulting path-aware search resolves failures or substantially reduces the number of evaluations required by the BFGS-SQP solver.</p></details> |  |
| **[Wide View Synthesis from Overlapping Perspective Cameras in Autonomous Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07081v1)** | 2026-11-04 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Multi-perspective-camera systems are widely adopted since they provide broad visual coverage for autonomous driving, motivating the synthesis of a unified wide view from spatially separated observations. In this work, we formulate wide view synthesis (WVS), which aims to synthesize a geometrically consistent wide view at a specified virtual viewpoint from multiple perspective cameras. Unlike conventional image stitching, WVS requires reasoning about 3D geometry and visibility to handle depth-dependent parallax and occlusions arising from spatially separated camera centers. While vanilla 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) enables high-fidelity view synthesis, its reliance on per-scene optimization limits its applicability to driving scenes. Furthermore, WVS introduces additional challenges, *e.g.*, peripheral stretching under perspective projection and photometric inconsistencies across cameras. To address these challenges, we propose **Wide-View Gaussian Splatting (WV-GS)**, a feed-forward Gaussian-splatting framework that synthesizes wide views without per-scene optimization. Specifically, we introduce Piecewise Perspective Rendering (PPR) to mitigate peripheral stretching through local perspective projections sharing a common camera center, and Spatial Affine Photometric Modeling (SAPM) to account for camera-dependent photometric variations during training. We further introduce **WideDrive**, a synthetic benchmark providing dense ground-truth wide views and adapt a LiDAR-projected sparse-reference protocol to real-world datasets. Extensive experiments on multiple datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of WV-GS for WVS. Dataset and code will be publicly released.</p></details> |  |
| **[A Mode-Unified, Continuous, and Biomimetic Control Strategy of a Robotic Lower Limb Prosthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19183v2)** | 2026-11-04 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Existing biomimetic lower-limb prosthesis controllers are commonly designed for discrete locomotion modes, requiring separate parameterizations for level walking, ramps, and stairs. We present a mode-unified prosthesis control framework that represents knee and ankle biomechanics continuously as functions of terrain slope and gait phase across those locomotion conditions. Locally optimized ascent and descent polynomial models were unified using radial basis function (RBF) interpolation, preserving the accuracy of the partitioned models while outperforming a global polynomial representation. The resulting biomechanics were transformed into continuous and slope-dependent impedance parameters and implemented within a single finite-state-machine (FSM) controller using bilateral sensing for gait-state estimation. Experiments with 10 individuals with transfemoral amputation demonstrated terrain-adaptive locomotion from level walking through ramps and stairs up to 35 degrees, including 13 degree ramp and 15 degree stair conditions absent from the underlying able-bodied (AB) dataset. Experimental results reproduced the principal slope-dependent biomechanical trends predicted by the unified model, with high agreement for knee-related measures (r-square = 0.82 - 0.99) and ankle kinematics (r-square= 0.78 - 0.96). These findings demonstrated that unified biomechanical parameterization enables a prosthesis control architecture across diverse terrain conditions while preserving key features of AB biomechanics without requiring independently tuned impedance parameter sets for each locomotion mode. To facilitate reproducibility and further development, the biomechanics modeling and impedance parameterization code is publicly available at https://github.com/kim2576.</p></details> | This work has been submitted to a research journal for possible publication. Copyright may be transferred without notice, after which this version may no longer be accessible |
| **[Ab Initio Accuracy Beyond the Cutoff: Distributed Message-Passing Equivariant Attention at Tens-of-Millions-of-Atom Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06122v1)** | 2026-11-04 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Molecular dynamics predicts molecular motion from atomic forces and trajectory integration. Density functional theory (DFT) provides first-principles forces, but its $O(N^3)$ cost limits simulations to thousands of atoms; empirical force fields scale to millions but miss bond breaking and charge transfer. Equivariant message-passing Transformers learn the ab initio potential at linear cost. Stacked layers propagate information beyond a single cutoff, giving four layers a receptive field up to 23Å. At this frontier, scale is limited by communication: layers exchange boundary features across devices, force backpropagation repeats the exchange, and moving atoms change neighbor graphs and tensor shapes. We present Tessera, a distributed runtime that fixes workload shapes between rebuilds. It sorts atoms along a space-filling curve into equal-count shards, ensuring identical atom counts and tensor shapes per rank. Peer sets scale as $K^{0.5}$, not $K$. A conservative neighbor skin preserves topology across updates, enabling each rebuild interval to replay as one CUDA Graph. Fused kernels, three-level neighbor search, and overlapped All-to-All complete it. On 512 GPUs, Tessera sustains 30.7M atoms at 89.7% weak-scaling efficiency (77--90% across three per-GPU loads); per-step time rises 1.11--1.29x as machine and problem grow 16x. Graph reuse cuts full-step time $1.87\times$. Across an $8\times$ device increase, neighbor-search cost grows $1.7\times$, versus $3.0\times$ for Morton order. In a live $10.24$M-atom salt solution, NVE energy drift is $0.42\,μ$eV/atom/ps over $10$ ps, and structure matches a serial reference. A 10.06M-atom Norovirus capsid sustains 100 ps of NVT at $\approx 8$ ps/day on 256 A100 GPUs. Over 200,000 steps, it has no broken bonds or anomalously close pairs and uses three compiled variants, with no graph breaks or eager fallbacks.</p></details> |  |
| **[Agentic Behavior Engineering: A Design-by-Contract Methodology for Specifying, Verifying, and Governing Enterprise AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05454v1)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Enterprise teams that deploy large language model (LLM) agents inherit a maintenance problem that traditional testing does not address. A specification-only behavior contract with stable rule identifiers states what the agent must do, must not do, and when it must abstain for lack of evidence; a lightweight scenario library turns each rule into concrete situations; and every engineering artifact (tests, runtime policies, telemetry) references the contract in one direction only, so that specification and implementation can change independently without drifting apart. The method organizes the work into five responsibilities (Specify, Ground, Control, Verify, Observe), treats missing evidence and a verified negative as different things, enforces authority outside the model, gates releases on per-scenario pass^k reliability rather than aggregate accuracy, and classifies every production run against the contract so that each finding routes to the asset that must change. We illustrate the method with an executable capacity-allocation boundary suite and a constructed worked example of a product-review approval workflow.</p></details> | 30 pages, 6 figures, 5 tables. Expanded version of the author's practitioner guide "Agentic behavior engineering" in the Microsoft Azure Architecture Center (CC BY 4.0): https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/architecture/ai-ml/guide/agentic-behavior-engineering |

| **[A Mode-Unified, Continuous, and Biomimetic Control Strategy of a Robotic Lower Limb Prosthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19183v2)** | 2026-11-04 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Existing biomimetic lower-limb prosthesis controllers are commonly designed for discrete locomotion modes, requiring separate parameterizations for level walking, ramps, and stairs. | **[Ab Initio Accuracy Beyond the Cutoff: Distributed Message-Passing Equivariant Attention at Tens-of-Millions-of-Atom Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06122v1)** | 2026-11-04 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Molecular dynamics predicts molecular motion from atomic forces and trajectory integration. A conservative neighbor skin preserves topology across updates, enabling each rebuild interval to replay as one CUDA Graph. Fused kernels, three-level neighbor search, and overlapped All-to-All complete it. On 512 GPUs, Tessera sustains 30.7M atoms at 89.7% weak-scaling efficiency (77--90% across three per-GPU loads); per-step time rises 1.11--1.29x as machine and problem grow 16x. Graph reuse cuts full-step time $1.87\times$. Across an $8\times$ device increase, neighbor-search cost grows $1.7\times$, versus $3.0\times$ for Morton order. | **[Agentic Behavior Engineering: A Design-by-Contract Methodology for Specifying, Verifying, and Governing Enterprise AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05454v1)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Enterprise teams that deploy large language model (LLM) agents inherit a maintenance problem that traditional testing does not address. This paper presents Agentic Behavior Engineering (ABE), a platform-agnostic methodology that treats observable agent behavior, rather than the prompt or the model, as the version-controlled engineering artifact. ABE adapts Design by Contract to non-deterministic systems. A specification-only behavior contract with stable rule identifiers states what the agent must do, must not do, and when it must abstain for lack of evidence; a lightweight scenario library turns each rule into concrete situations; and every engineering artifact (tests, runtime policies, telemetry) references the contract in one direction only, so that specification and implementation can change independently without drifting apart. The method organizes the work into five responsibilities (Specify, Ground, Control, Verify, Observe), treats missing evidence and a verified negative as different things, enforces authority outside the model, gates releases on per-scenario pass^k reliability rather than aggregate accuracy, and classifies every production run against the contract so that each finding routes to the asset that must change. We illustrate the method with an executable capacity-allocation boundary suite and a constructed worked example of a product-review approval workflow.</p></details> | 30 pages, 6 figures, 5 tables. Our proof extends the Bochner method through an abstract inequality for orthogonal projections on Hilbert spaces. As an application, we prove that Glauber dynamics for the $O(N)$ model, with $N\geq 2$, arbitrary external fields, and couplings of either sign, has spectral gap at least $(1-\&#124;J_{\mathrm{abs}}\&#124;_{\mathrm{op}}/N)/n$, where $J$ is the interaction matrix and $J_{\mathrm{abs}}=(&#124;J_{ij}&#124;)$. We also obtain spectral bounds for swap walks and local spectral expansion of partite simplicial complexes. For complexes formed from disjoint groups of ranks in random flags of subspaces over $\mathbb F_q$, we obtain a one-sided local spectral expansion bound of $2\sqrt q/(q+1)$. This bound is independent of the number and sizes of each group, and is nontrivial for every prime power $q$. Finally, for random forests $F$ of any finite graph with arbitrary positive edge weights, we prove that \[ \Pr(e,f\in F)\le \frac{4}{3} \Pr(e\in F)\Pr(f\in F) \] for distinct edges $e,f$, improving the factor 2 supplied by general log-concavity bounds for matroid independent sets. | **[Data-driven candidate structure ensemble generation beyond the convex hull with crystALL approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02584v1)** | 2026-11-02 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Real materials consist of multiple stable or metastable states under real-world conditions, existing as statistical ergodic ensembles of configurations rather than ideal crystals. Yet, data-driven computational materials discovery increasingly biases itself toward finding "new stable structures" from ground-state DFT calculations alone. This limits our long-term ability to discover materials whose properties emerge from metastable phases, to explain experimental observations, or to model thermodynamic behavior comprehensively. Predicting ergodic sets of atomic configurations of known and hypothetical solids is a fundamental challenge in theoretical crystallography, approached via many complementary methods. We discuss this conceptually and introduce crystALL, a pragmatic and powerful data-driven approach to discovering ensembles beyond the convex hull, embracing structural diversity over stability alone, enabling robust experiment matching in cases evading traditional methods. crystALL leverages 5 million relaxed structures from OPTIMADE and MPDD to rapidly generate a comprehensive candidate set by: (1) species substitutions; (2) ML feature-space analysis with pySIPFENN; (3) representative structure selection via clustering and energy-minimization or property matching; and (4) construction of the final candidate ensemble for experimental or ab initio validation. We also uniquely address the common challenge of experimental composition uncertainties spanning many integer stoichiometries by new efficient searches across anonymized compositional ranges rather than fixed formulas. Overall, crystALL's ensemble-focused philosophy provides critical advantages over the prevailing convex-hull-searching paradigm, which often fails to account for key experimental observations and increases AI/ML discovery bias over time - akin to model collapse in LLMs retrained on iteratively generated content.</p></details> | 19 pages, 7 figures, software repository https://github.com/PhasesResearchLab/crystALL |
| **[TRUCO: A Scalable Lock-Free Algorithm for Parallel Contour Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37297v2)** | 2026-11-01 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Extracting closed pixel contours from a binary image, together with the hierarchy that records which contours enclose which, is a basic step in many computer vision pipelines, from fiducial marker detection to the vectorization of segmentation masks.
