The method rests on two ideas. First, the image is divided into horizontal stripes, one per thread, and two rules, row-local writes and geometric contour ownership, let each thread trace complete contours independently of the others. Second, the tracing of the contours is separated from the computation of the hierarchy, which is recovered afterwards from one reference per contour; the trace therefore stores no contour numbers in the label image, which also makes it more efficient than that of the original algorithm when a single thread is used. We prove that the output is deterministic and identical to that of Suzuki--Abe, and that the algorithm is free of data races. Experiments on synthetic images, high-resolution photographs and segmentation masks, on a desktop and a server CPU, show that TRUCO scales with the number of threads and is several times faster than the fastest sequential methods, with and without the hierarchy. With a single thread, it is already faster than Suzuki--Abe.</p></details> |  |
| **[Improvise, Adapt, Overcome: An On-The-Fly Multifidelity Algorithm for Efficient Machine Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02662v2)** | 2026-10-31 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Machine learning has accelerated quantum chemistry but is hindered by the prohibitive cost of generating high fidelity training data. Multifidelity machine learning (MFML) mitigates this overhead by systematically combining abundant low fidelity data with sparse high fidelity data. In spite of its success, standard MFML schemes rely on pre-defined scaling factors to determine sparse data ratio across fidelities, often generating redundant multifidelity data resulting in a loss of efficiency. Here, we introduce an adaptive on-the-fly multifidelity framework for machine learning that autonomously determines training dataset composition. By dynamically querying training samples at each fidelity, the algorithm saturates model accuracy at lower fidelities before moving up to more expensive reference calculations. We benchmark the novel adaptive-MFML across diverse chemical properties including theoretical best estimate ab initio potential energy surfaces, golden standard coupled cluster energies, and the more chemically challenging excitation energies. In our numerical experiments we show that our adaptive algorithm reduces data generation costs by up to a factor of 20 compared to single fidelity methods and improves upon standard MFML by up to a factor of 3. The mitigation of data redundancy establishes a high-accuracy low-cost pathway for sustainable cost-aware machine learning in quantum chemistry.</p></details> | Supplementary Information added within the main text towards the end; updated several things in main text and added several things in SI |

| **[Ground-Wing: A Parametric 3D Wing-in-Ground-Effect Dataset for Benchmarking Neural Surrogate Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04571v2)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This paper presents a CFD dataset of a highly parametrized 3D wing operating in ground effect, designed for benchmarking neural surrogate models for aerodynamic prediction, using a wing shape and flow conditions typically encountered in motorsport applications. The dataset comprises 770 RANS simulations of 194 unique wing configurations, sampled across 27 airfoil profile variants and 4 distances from the ground, spanning variations in wing sweep, chord, taper, angle of attack, and tip twist. Six neural network architectures representing distinct paradigms --- convolutional (DoMINO), graph-based (X-MeshGraphNet), transformer-based (AB-UPT, GeoTransolver, GAOT), and JEPA-based (AeroJEPA) --- are evaluated on their ability to predict surface pressure coefficient and wall shear stress distributions, from which integral drag and lift coefficients are recovered. The dataset is intended to be used as a challenging benchmark for surrogate CFD models, due to the uniqueness of the ground effect being absent from other wing and airfoil datasets. The dataset is available for download at https://huggingface.co/datasets/andreacenzato/Ground-Wing</p></details> |  |

We also analyze a Thompson sampling alternative (\texttt{TS-SID-iVAR}) as a higher variance Monte Carlo variant. Despite the unknown target, under mild conditions, we establish that the terminal prediction error vanishes with high probability, and provide a tighter average-case guarantee. We demonstrate consistent improvements over existing approaches in this setting on synthetic benchmarks and real-world PES modeling and drug discovery tasks. Our code is available at https://github.com/TsingQAQ/SIDAL</p></details> |  |

In spite of its success, standard MFML schemes rely on pre-defined scaling factors to determine sparse data ratio across fidelities, often generating redundant multifidelity data resulting in a loss of efficiency. Here, we introduce an adaptive on-the-fly multifidelity framework for machine learning that autonomously determines training dataset composition. By dynamically querying training samples at each fidelity, the algorithm saturates model accuracy at lower fidelities before moving up to more expensive reference calculations. We benchmark the novel adaptive-MFML across diverse chemical properties including theoretical best estimate ab initio potential energy surfaces, golden standard coupled cluster energies, and the more chemically challenging excitation energies. In our numerical experiments we show that our adaptive algorithm reduces data generation costs by up to a factor of 20 compared to single fidelity methods and improves upon standard MFML by up to a factor of 3. The mitigation of data redundancy establishes a high-accuracy low-cost pathway for sustainable cost-aware machine learning in quantum chemistry.</p></details> | Supplementary Information added within the main text towards the end; updated several things in main text and added several things in SI |

| **[Ground-Wing: A Parametric 3D Wing-in-Ground-Effect Dataset for Benchmarking Neural Surrogate Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04571v2)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This paper presents a CFD dataset of a highly parametrized 3D wing operating in ground effect, designed for benchmarking neural surrogate models for aerodynamic prediction, using a wing shape and flow conditions typically encountered in motorsport applications. The dataset comprises 770 RANS simulations of 194 unique wing configurations, sampled across 27 airfoil profile variants and 4 distances from the ground, spanning variations in wing sweep, chord, taper, angle of attack, and tip twist. Six neural network architectures representing distinct paradigms --- convolutional (DoMINO), graph-based (X-MeshGraphNet), transformer-based (AB-UPT, GeoTransolver, GAOT), and JEPA-based (AeroJEPA) --- are evaluated on their ability to predict surface pressure coefficient and wall shear stress distributions, from which integral drag and lift coefficients are recovered. The dataset is intended to be used as a challenging benchmark for surrogate CFD models, due to the uniqueness of the ground effect being absent from other wing and airfoil datasets. The dataset is available for download at https://huggingface.co/datasets/andreacenzato/Ground-Wing</p></details> |  |
| **[Active Learning for Gaussian Process Regression Under Self-Induced Boltzmann Weights](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.10654v2)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We consider the active learning problem where the goal is to learn an unknown function with low prediction error under an unknown Boltzmann distribution induced by the function itself. This self-induced weighting arises naturally in problems such as potential energy surface (PES) modeling in computational chemistry, yet poses unique challenges as the target distribution is unknown and its partition function is intractable. We propose \texttt{AB-SID-iVAR}, a Gaussian Process-based acquisition function that approximates the intractable Bayesian target distribution in closed form while avoiding partition function estimation, and is applicable to both discrete and continuous input domains. We also analyze a Thompson sampling alternative (\texttt{TS-SID-iVAR}) as a higher variance Monte Carlo variant. Despite the unknown target, under mild conditions, we establish that the terminal prediction error vanishes with high probability, and provide a tighter average-case guarantee. We demonstrate consistent improvements over existing approaches in this setting on synthetic benchmarks and real-world PES modeling and drug discovery tasks. Our code is available at https://github.com/TsingQAQ/SIDAL</p></details> |  |
| **[Singular Bayesian Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.00387v5)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Bayesian neural networks promise calibrated uncertainty but require $O(mn)$ parameters for standard mean-field Gaussian posteriors. We argue this cost is often unnecessary, particularly when weight matrices exhibit fast singular value decay. By parameterizing weights as $W = AB^{\top}$ with $A \in \mathbb{R}^{m \times r}$, $B \in \mathbb{R}^{n \times r}$, we induce a posterior that is \emph{singular} with respect to the Lebesgue measure, concentrating on the rank-$r$ manifold. This singularity captures structured weight correlations through shared latent factors, geometrically distinct from mean-field's independence assumption. We derive PAC-Bayes generalization bounds whose complexity term scales as $\sqrt{r(m+n)}$ instead of $\sqrt{m n}$, and prove loss bounds that decompose the error into optimization and rank-induced bias using the Eckart-Young-Mirsky theorem. We further adapt recent Gaussian complexity bounds for low-rank deterministic networks to Bayesian predictive means. Empirically, across MLPs, LSTMs, and Transformers on standard benchmarks, our method achieves competitive predictive performance while using up to $33\times$ fewer parameters than 5-member Deep Ensembles. It substantially improves OOD detection and often improves calibration relative to mean-field and perturbation baselines, while Deep Ensembles can still be stronger on in-distribution likelihood-based metrics.</p></details> | 8 pages Main text, 53 pages Appendix, 20 figures Proceedings of the 43 rd International Conference on Machine Learning (ICML 2026) |
| **[Branch-Extended Automatic Differentiation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08637v1)** | 2026-11-05 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We present branch-extended automatic differentiation (BEAD), a software package that provides information about inactive branches of piecewise-differentiable functions, intended for use in nonsmooth optimization algorithms. BEAD is implemented in Python, building on the popular JAX framework. For supported nonsmooth operations such as abs and max, BEAD identifies alternative branches within tolerance from the given input, and computes gradients of the resulting branches extended to the given input. Such information has been previously used for robust optimization methods, but has required error-prone and tedious manual differentiation, unlike BEAD, which is fully automated for functions written in NumPy-like JAX code. Our experiments show that our approach is as accurate as manually written derivative code, and offers more flexibility in modifying the function and choosing between branch-discovery rules. While we were originally motivated by the manifold sampling algorithm for nonsmooth composite objectives, we also integrate BEAD branch-extension gradients into a nonsmooth BFGS-SQP solver. On two small nonsmooth test problems, the resulting path-aware search resolves failures or substantially reduces the number of evaluations required by the BFGS-SQP solver.</p></details> |  |
| **[A Mode-Unified, Continuous, and Biomimetic Control Strategy of a Robotic Lower Limb Prosthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19183v2)** | 2026-11-04 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Existing biomimetic lower-limb prosthesis controllers are commonly designed for discrete locomotion modes, requiring separate parameterizations for level walking, ramps, and stairs. We present a mode-unified prosthesis control framework that represents knee and ankle biomechanics continuously as functions of terrain slope and gait phase across those locomotion conditions. Locally optimized ascent and descent polynomial models were unified using radial basis function (RBF) interpolation, preserving the accuracy of the partitioned models while outperforming a global polynomial representation. The resulting biomechanics were transformed into continuous and slope-dependent impedance parameters and implemented within a single finite-state-machine (FSM) controller using bilateral sensing for gait-state estimation. Experiments with 10 individuals with transfemoral amputation demonstrated terrain-adaptive locomotion from level walking through ramps and stairs up to 35 degrees, including 13 degree ramp and 15 degree stair conditions absent from the underlying able-bodied (AB) dataset. Experimental results reproduced the principal slope-dependent biomechanical trends predicted by the unified model, with high agreement for knee-related measures (r-square = 0.82 - 0.99) and ankle kinematics (r-square= 0.78 - 0.96). These findings demonstrated that unified biomechanical parameterization enables a prosthesis control architecture across diverse terrain conditions while preserving key features of AB biomechanics without requiring independently tuned impedance parameter sets for each locomotion mode. To facilitate reproducibility and further development, the biomechanics modeling and impedance parameterization code is publicly available at https://github.com/kim2576.</p></details> | This work has been submitted to a research journal for possible publication. Copyright may be transferred without notice, after which this version may no longer be accessible |
| **[Ab Initio Accuracy Beyond the Cutoff: Distributed Message-Passing Equivariant Attention at Tens-of-Millions-of-Atom Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06122v1)** | 2026-11-04 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Molecular dynamics predicts molecular motion from atomic forces and trajectory integration. Density functional theory (DFT) provides first-principles forces, but its $O(N^3)$ cost limits simulations to thousands of atoms; empirical force fields scale to millions but miss bond breaking and charge transfer. Equivariant message-passing Transformers learn the ab initio potential at linear cost. Stacked layers propagate information beyond a single cutoff, giving four layers a receptive field up to 23Å. At this frontier, scale is limited by communication: layers exchange boundary features across devices, force backpropagation repeats the exchange, and moving atoms change neighbor graphs and tensor shapes. We present Tessera, a distributed runtime that fixes workload shapes between rebuilds. It sorts atoms along a space-filling curve into equal-count shards, ensuring identical atom counts and tensor shapes per rank. Peer sets scale as $K^{0.5}$, not $K$. A conservative neighbor skin preserves topology across updates, enabling each rebuild interval to replay as one CUDA Graph. Fused kernels, three-level neighbor search, and overlapped All-to-All complete it. On 512 GPUs, Tessera sustains 30.7M atoms at 89.7% weak-scaling efficiency (77--90% across three per-GPU loads); per-step time rises 1.11--1.29x as machine and problem grow 16x. Graph reuse cuts full-step time $1.87\times$. Across an $8\times$ device increase, neighbor-search cost grows $1.7\times$, versus $3.0\times$ for Morton order. In a live $10.24$M-atom salt solution, NVE energy drift is $0.42\,μ$eV/atom/ps over $10$ ps, and structure matches a serial reference. A 10.06M-atom Norovirus capsid sustains 100 ps of NVT at $\approx 8$ ps/day on 256 A100 GPUs. Over 200,000 steps, it has no broken bonds or anomalously close pairs and uses three compiled variants, with no graph breaks or eager fallbacks.</p></details> |  |
| **[Agentic Behavior Engineering: A Design-by-Contract Methodology for Specifying, Verifying, and Governing Enterprise AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05454v1)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Enterprise teams that deploy large language model (LLM) agents inherit a maintenance problem that traditional testing does not address. The model is non-deterministic, but the larger risk is that most of what changes an agent's behavior (a retired model version, a revised policy, an upstream schema change, a prompt edit meant to fix one case) originates outside the owning team and is discovered as a production defect rather than caught as a regression. This paper presents Agentic Behavior Engineering (ABE), a platform-agnostic methodology that treats observable agent behavior, rather than the prompt or the model, as the version-controlled engineering artifact. ABE adapts Design by Contract to non-deterministic systems. A specification-only behavior contract with stable rule identifiers states what the agent must do, must not do, and when it must abstain for lack of evidence; a lightweight scenario library turns each rule into concrete situations; and every engineering artifact (tests, runtime policies, telemetry) references the contract in one direction only, so that specification and implementation can change independently without drifting apart. The method organizes the work into five responsibilities (Specify, Ground, Control, Verify, Observe), treats missing evidence and a verified negative as different things, enforces authority outside the model, gates releases on per-scenario pass^k reliability rather than aggregate accuracy, and classifies every production run against the contract so that each finding routes to the asset that must change. We illustrate the method with an executable capacity-allocation boundary suite and a constructed worked example of a product-review approval workflow.</p></details> | 30 pages, 6 figures, 5 tables. Expanded version of the author's practitioner guide "Agentic behavior engineering" in the Microsoft Azure Architecture Center (CC BY 4.0): https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/architecture/ai-ml/guide/agentic-behavior-engineering |
| **[Beyond the Bochner Method: Optimal Spectral Gaps for Block Dynamics of Spin Systems like the $O(N)$ Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05228v1)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Let $μ$ be a probability distribution on a spin system with $n$ sites and possibly infinite spin spaces. We prove local-to-global spectral-gap bounds for block Glauber dynamics. Given a symmetric, entrywise nonnegative matrix $A$ with zero diagonal and $λ_{\max}(A)&lt;1$ encoding local spectral bounds for a spin system, we derive global spectral-gap bounds for block dynamics on its marginal distributions. These bounds are sharp: the Gaussian system with covariance $(I-A)^{-1}$ attains equality for every choice of disjoint blocks. Our proof extends the Bochner method through an abstract inequality for orthogonal projections on Hilbert spaces. As an application, we prove that Glauber dynamics for the $O(N)$ model, with $N\geq 2$, arbitrary external fields, and couplings of either sign, has spectral gap at least $(1-\&#124;J_{\mathrm{abs}}\&#124;_{\mathrm{op}}/N)/n$, where $J$ is the interaction matrix and $J_{\mathrm{abs}}=(&#124;J_{ij}&#124;)$. We also obtain spectral bounds for swap walks and local spectral expansion of partite simplicial complexes. For complexes formed from disjoint groups of ranks in random flags of subspaces over $\mathbb F_q$, we obtain a one-sided local spectral expansion bound of $2\sqrt q/(q+1)$. This bound is independent of the number and sizes of each group, and is nontrivial for every prime power $q$. Finally, for random forests $F$ of any finite graph with arbitrary positive edge weights, we prove that \[ \Pr(e,f\in F)\le \frac{4}{3} \Pr(e\in F)\Pr(f\in F) \] for distinct edges $e,f$, improving the factor 2 supplied by general log-concavity bounds for matroid independent sets. The argument combines our extended Bochner method with the analysis of an auxiliary real stable polynomial.</p></details> | 42 pages |
| **[Data-driven candidate structure ensemble generation beyond the convex hull with crystALL approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02584v1)** | 2026-11-02 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Real materials consist of multiple stable or metastable states under real-world conditions, existing as statistical ergodic ensembles of configurations rather than ideal crystals. Yet, data-driven computational materials discovery increasingly biases itself toward finding "new stable structures" from ground-state DFT calculations alone. This limits our long-term ability to discover materials whose properties emerge from metastable phases, to explain experimental observations, or to model thermodynamic behavior comprehensively. Predicting ergodic sets of atomic configurations of known and hypothetical solids is a fundamental challenge in theoretical crystallography, approached via many complementary methods. We discuss this conceptually and introduce crystALL, a pragmatic and powerful data-driven approach to discovering ensembles beyond the convex hull, embracing structural diversity over stability alone, enabling robust experiment matching in cases evading traditional methods. crystALL leverages 5 million relaxed structures from OPTIMADE and MPDD to rapidly generate a comprehensive candidate set by: (1) species substitutions; (2) ML feature-space analysis with pySIPFENN; (3) representative structure selection via clustering and energy-minimization or property matching; and (4) construction of the final candidate ensemble for experimental or ab initio validation. We also uniquely address the common challenge of experimental composition uncertainties spanning many integer stoichiometries by new efficient searches across anonymized compositional ranges rather than fixed formulas. Overall, crystALL's ensemble-focused philosophy provides critical advantages over the prevailing convex-hull-searching paradigm, which often fails to account for key experimental observations and increases AI/ML discovery bias over time - akin to model collapse in LLMs retrained on iteratively generated content.</p></details> | 19 pages, 7 figures, software repository https://github.com/PhasesResearchLab/crystALL |
| **[TRUCO: A Scalable Lock-Free Algorithm for Parallel Contour Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37297v2)** | 2026-11-01 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Extracting closed pixel contours from a binary image, together with the hierarchy that records which contours enclose which, is a basic step in many computer vision pipelines, from fiducial marker detection to the vectorization of segmentation masks. The reference algorithm, by Suzuki and Abe, is inherently sequential: each contour is found by a raster scan that depends on the labels left by all previous contours, and its parent is obtained from the last contour met by the scan. This paper presents TRUCO (Threaded Raster Unrestricted Contour Ownership), a multi-core algorithm that returns exactly the output of Suzuki--Abe (the same contours, in the same order, with the same hierarchy) without locks, read-modify-write operations or a stitching phase. The method rests on two ideas. First, the image is divided into horizontal stripes, one per thread, and two rules, row-local writes and geometric contour ownership, let each thread trace complete contours independently of the others. Second, the tracing of the contours is separated from the computation of the hierarchy, which is recovered afterwards from one reference per contour; the trace therefore stores no contour numbers in the label image, which also makes it more efficient than that of the original algorithm when a single thread is used. We prove that the output is deterministic and identical to that of Suzuki--Abe, and that the algorithm is free of data races. Experiments on synthetic images, high-resolution photographs and segmentation masks, on a desktop and a server CPU, show that TRUCO scales with the number of threads and is several times faster than the fastest sequential methods, with and without the hierarchy. With a single thread, it is already faster than Suzuki--Abe.</p></details> |  |

| **[Data-driven candidate structure ensemble generation beyond the convex hull with crystALL approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02584v1)** | 2026-11-02 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Real materials consist of multiple stable or metastable states under real-world conditions, existing as statistical ergodic ensembles of configurations rather than ideal crystals. Yet, data-driven computational materials discovery increasingly biases itself toward finding "new stable structures" from ground-state DFT calculations alone. This limits our long-term ability to discover materials whose properties emerge from metastable phases, to explain experimental observations, or to model thermodynamic behavior comprehensively. Predicting ergodic sets of atomic configurations of known and hypothetical solids is a fundamental challenge in theoretical crystallography, approached via many complementary methods. We discuss this conceptually and introduce crystALL, a pragmatic and powerful data-driven approach to discovering ensembles beyond the convex hull, embracing structural diversity over stability alone, enabling robust experiment matching in cases evading traditional methods. crystALL leverages 5 million relaxed structures from OPTIMADE and MPDD to rapidly generate a comprehensive candidate set by: (1) species substitutions; (2) ML feature-space analysis with pySIPFENN; (3) representative structure selection via clustering and energy-minimization or property matching; and (4) construction of the final candidate ensemble for experimental or ab initio validation. We also uniquely address the common challenge of experimental composition uncertainties spanning many integer stoichiometries by new efficient searches across anonymized compositional ranges rather than fixed formulas. Overall, crystALL's ensemble-focused philosophy provides critical advantages over the prevailing convex-hull-searching paradigm, which often fails to account for key experimental observations and increases AI/ML discovery bias over time - akin to model collapse in LLMs retrained on iteratively generated content.</p></details> | 19 pages, 7 figures, software repository https://github.com/PhasesResearchLab/crystALL |
| **[TRUCO: A Scalable Lock-Free Algorithm for Parallel Contour Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37297v2)** | 2026-11-01 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Extracting closed pixel contours from a binary image, together with the hierarchy that records which contours enclose which, is a basic step in many computer vision pipelines, from fiducial marker detection to the vectorization of segmentation masks. The reference algorithm, by Suzuki and Abe, is inherently sequential: each contour is found by a raster scan that depends on the labels left by all previous contours, and its parent is obtained from the last contour met by the scan. The method rests on two ideas. First, the image is divided into horizontal stripes, one per thread, and two rules, row-local writes and geometric contour ownership, let each thread trace complete contours independently of the others. Second, the tracing of the contours is separated from the computation of the hierarchy, which is recovered afterwards from one reference per contour; the trace therefore stores no contour numbers in the label image, which also makes it more efficient than that of the original algorithm when a single thread is used. We prove that the output is deterministic and identical to that of Suzuki--Abe, and that the algorithm is free of data races. Experiments on synthetic images, high-resolution photographs and segmentation masks, on a desktop and a server CPU, show that TRUCO scales with the number of threads and is several times faster than the fastest sequential methods, with and without the hierarchy. With a single thread, it is already faster than Suzuki--Abe.</p></details> |  |
| **[Improvise, Adapt, Overcome: An On-The-Fly Multifidelity Algorithm for Efficient Machine Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02662v2)** | 2026-10-31 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Machine learning has accelerated quantum chemistry but is hindered by the prohibitive cost of generating high fidelity training data. Multifidelity machine learning (MFML) mitigates this overhead by systematically combining abundant low fidelity data with sparse high fidelity data. In spite of its success, standard MFML schemes rely on pre-defined scaling factors to determine sparse data ratio across fidelities, often generating redundant multifidelity data resulting in a loss of efficiency. Here, we introduce an adaptive on-the-fly multifidelity framework for machine learning that autonomously determines training dataset composition. By dynamically querying training samples at each fidelity, the algorithm saturates model accuracy at lower fidelities before moving up to more expensive reference calculations. We benchmark the novel adaptive-MFML across diverse chemical properties including theoretical best estimate ab initio potential energy surfaces, golden standard coupled cluster energies, and the more chemically challenging excitation energies. In our numerical experiments we show that our adaptive algorithm reduces data generation costs by up to a factor of 20 compared to single fidelity methods and improves upon standard MFML by up to a factor of 3. The mitigation of data redundancy establishes a high-accuracy low-cost pathway for sustainable cost-aware machine learning in quantum chemistry.</p></details> | Supplementary Information added within the main text towards the end; updated several things in main text and added several things in SI |

| **[Ground-Wing: A Parametric 3D Wing-in-Ground-Effect Dataset for Benchmarking Neural Surrogate Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04571v2)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This paper presents a CFD dataset of a highly parametrized 3D wing operating in ground effect, designed for benchmarking neural surrogate models for aerodynamic prediction, using a wing shape and flow conditions typically encountered in motorsport applications. The dataset comprises 770 RANS simulations of 194 unique wing configurations, sampled across 27 airfoil profile variants and 4 distances from the ground, spanning variations in wing sweep, chord, taper, angle of attack, and tip twist. Six neural network architectures representing distinct paradigms --- convolutional (DoMINO), graph-based (X-MeshGraphNet), transformer-based (AB-UPT, GeoTransolver, GAOT), and JEPA-based (AeroJEPA) --- are evaluated on their ability to predict surface pressure coefficient and wall shear stress distributions, from which integral drag and lift coefficients are recovered. The dataset is intended to be used as a challenging benchmark for surrogate CFD models, due to the uniqueness of the ground effect being absent from other wing and airfoil datasets. The dataset is available for download at https://huggingface.co/datasets/andreacenzato/Ground-Wing</p></details> |  |
| **[Active Learning for Gaussian Process Regression Under Self-Induced Boltzmann Weights](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.10654v2)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We consider the active learning problem where the goal is to learn an unknown function with low prediction error under an unknown Boltzmann distribution induced by the function itself. This self-induced weighting arises naturally in problems such as potential energy surface (PES) modeling in computational chemistry, yet poses unique challenges as the target distribution is unknown and its partition function is intractable. We propose \texttt{AB-SID-iVAR}, a Gaussian Process-based acquisition function that approximates the intractable Bayesian target distribution in closed form while avoiding partition function estimation, and is applicable to both discrete and continuous input domains. We also analyze a Thompson sampling alternative (\texttt{TS-SID-iVAR}) as a higher variance Monte Carlo variant. Despite the unknown target, under mild conditions, we establish that the terminal prediction error vanishes with high probability, and provide a tighter average-case guarantee. We demonstrate consistent improvements over existing approaches in this setting on synthetic benchmarks and real-world PES modeling and drug discovery tasks. Our code is available at https://github.com/TsingQAQ/SIDAL</p></details> |  |
| **[Singular Bayesian Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.00387v5)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Bayesian neural networks promise calibrated uncertainty but require $O(mn)$ parameters for standard mean-field Gaussian posteriors. We argue this cost is often unnecessary, particularly when weight matrices exhibit fast singular value decay. By parameterizing weights as $W = AB^{\top}$ with $A \in \mathbb{R}^{m \times r}$, $B \in \mathbb{R}^{n \times r}$, we induce a posterior that is \emph{singular} with respect to the Lebesgue measure, concentrating on the rank-$r$ manifold. This singularity captures structured weight correlations through shared latent factors, geometrically distinct from mean-field's independence assumption. We derive PAC-Bayes generalization bounds whose complexity term scales as $\sqrt{r(m+n)}$ instead of $\sqrt{m n}$, and prove loss bounds that decompose the error into optimization and rank-induced bias using the Eckart-Young-Mirsky theorem. We further adapt recent Gaussian complexity bounds for low-rank deterministic networks to Bayesian predictive means. Empirically, across MLPs, LSTMs, and Transformers on standard benchmarks, our method achieves competitive predictive performance while using up to $33\times$ fewer parameters than 5-member Deep Ensembles. It substantially improves OOD detection and often improves calibration relative to mean-field and perturbation baselines, while Deep Ensembles can still be stronger on in-distribution likelihood-based metrics.</p></details> | 8 pages Main text, 53 pages Appendix, 20 figures Proceedings of the 43 rd International Conference on Machine Learning (ICML 2026) |
| **[Branch-Extended Automatic Differentiation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08637v1)** | 2026-11-05 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We present branch-extended automatic differentiation (BEAD), a software package that provides information about inactive branches of piecewise-differentiable functions, intended for use in nonsmooth optimization algorithms. BEAD is implemented in Python, building on the popular JAX framework. For supported nonsmooth operations such as abs and max, BEAD identifies alternative branches within tolerance from the given input, and computes gradients of the resulting branches extended to the given input. Such information has been previously used for robust optimization methods, but has required error-prone and tedious manual differentiation, unlike BEAD, which is fully automated for functions written in NumPy-like JAX code. Our experiments show that our approach is as accurate as manually written derivative code, and offers more flexibility in modifying the function and choosing between branch-discovery rules. While we were originally motivated by the manifold sampling algorithm for nonsmooth composite objectives, we also integrate BEAD branch-extension gradients into a nonsmooth BFGS-SQP solver. On two small nonsmooth test problems, the resulting path-aware search resolves failures or substantially reduces the number of evaluations required by the BFGS-SQP solver.</p></details> |  |
| **[XM4D: 4D Reconstruction through Certifiable Geometric Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07902v1)** | 2026-11-05 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Accurate 4D reconstruction remains challenging: jointly estimating camera motion, scene geometry, and non-rigid deformation entails highly nonconvex optimization, making global optimality difficult to guarantee. We present XM4D, a framework for accurate and efficient 4D reconstruction built upon certifiably globally optimal geometric optimization. By separating the estimation of camera motion from non-rigid deformation, we formulate both stages as structured quadratically constrained quadratic programs (QCQP) that admit semidefinite programming (SDP) relaxations and verifiable global optimality certificates. For camera motion estimation, we lift static point tracks into 3D using foundational depth prediction models and formulate a scaled bundle adjustment problem that jointly estimates camera poses, depth scales, and static landmarks. By analytically eliminating translations and landmarks, we reduce the problem to a QCQP over scaled rotations. For non-rigid reconstruction, we represent dynamic motion using an embedded deformation graph (EDG) and show that its tracking, rigidity, and temporal regularization objectives similarly reduce to a rotation-only QCQP. Both problems admit SDP relaxations that we solve efficiently with a GPU-accelerated Burer-Monteiro solver based on Riemannian optimization. The recovered geometry further initializes dynamic Gaussian splatting for photometric refinement. Extensive experiments across seven benchmarks demonstrate accurate camera motion estimation on both static and dynamic scenes, efficient optimization that scales to thousands of frames, and state-of-the-art novel-view rendering quality.</p></details> |  |
| **[A Mode-Unified, Continuous, and Biomimetic Control Strategy of a Robotic Lower Limb Prosthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19183v2)** | 2026-11-04 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Existing biomimetic lower-limb prosthesis controllers are commonly designed for discrete locomotion modes, requiring separate parameterizations for level walking, ramps, and stairs. We present a mode-unified prosthesis control framework that represents knee and ankle biomechanics continuously as functions of terrain slope and gait phase across those locomotion conditions. Locally optimized ascent and descent polynomial models were unified using radial basis function (RBF) interpolation, preserving the accuracy of the partitioned models while outperforming a global polynomial representation. The resulting biomechanics were transformed into continuous and slope-dependent impedance parameters and implemented within a single finite-state-machine (FSM) controller using bilateral sensing for gait-state estimation. Experiments with 10 individuals with transfemoral amputation demonstrated terrain-adaptive locomotion from level walking through ramps and stairs up to 35 degrees, including 13 degree ramp and 15 degree stair conditions absent from the underlying able-bodied (AB) dataset. Experimental results reproduced the principal slope-dependent biomechanical trends predicted by the unified model, with high agreement for knee-related measures (r-square = 0.82 - 0.99) and ankle kinematics (r-square= 0.78 - 0.96). These findings demonstrated that unified biomechanical parameterization enables a prosthesis control architecture across diverse terrain conditions while preserving key features of AB biomechanics without requiring independently tuned impedance parameter sets for each locomotion mode. To facilitate reproducibility and further development, the biomechanics modeling and impedance parameterization code is publicly available at https://github.com/kim2576.</p></details> | This work has been submitted to a research journal for possible publication. | **[Ab Initio Accuracy Beyond the Cutoff: Distributed Message-Passing Equivariant Attention at Tens-of-Millions-of-Atom Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06122v1)** | 2026-11-04 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Molecular dynamics predicts molecular motion from atomic forces and trajectory integration. Density functional theory (DFT) provides first-principles forces, but its $O(N^3)$ cost limits simulations to thousands of atoms; empirical force fields scale to millions but miss bond breaking and charge transfer. Equivariant message-passing Transformers learn the ab initio potential at linear cost. Stacked layers propagate information beyond a single cutoff, giving four layers a receptive field up to 23Å. At this frontier, scale is limited by communication: layers exchange boundary features across devices, force backpropagation repeats the exchange, and moving atoms change neighbor graphs and tensor shapes. We present Tessera, a distributed runtime that fixes workload shapes between rebuilds. It sorts atoms along a space-filling curve into equal-count shards, ensuring identical atom counts and tensor shapes per rank. Peer sets scale as $K^{0.5}$, not $K$. A conservative neighbor skin preserves topology across updates, enabling each rebuild interval to replay as one CUDA Graph. Fused kernels, three-level neighbor search, and overlapped All-to-All complete it. On 512 GPUs, Tessera sustains 30.7M atoms at 89.7% weak-scaling efficiency (77--90% across three per-GPU loads); per-step time rises 1.11--1.29x as machine and problem grow 16x. Graph reuse cuts full-step time $1.87\times$. Across an $8\times$ device increase, neighbor-search cost grows $1.7\times$, versus $3.0\times$ for Morton order. In a live $10.24$M-atom salt solution, NVE energy drift is $0.42\,μ$eV/atom/ps over $10$ ps, and structure matches a serial reference. A 10.06M-atom Norovirus capsid sustains 100 ps of NVT at $\approx 8$ ps/day on 256 A100 GPUs. Over 200,000 steps, it has no broken bonds or anomalously close pairs and uses three compiled variants, with no graph breaks or eager fallbacks.</p></details> |  |
| **[Agentic Behavior Engineering: A Design-by-Contract Methodology for Specifying, Verifying, and Governing Enterprise AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05454v1)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Enterprise teams that deploy large language model (LLM) agents inherit a maintenance problem that traditional testing does not address. This paper presents Agentic Behavior Engineering (ABE), a platform-agnostic methodology that treats observable agent behavior, rather than the prompt or the model, as the version-controlled engineering artifact. The method organizes the work into five responsibilities (Specify, Ground, Control, Verify, Observe), treats missing evidence and a verified negative as different things, enforces authority outside the model, gates releases on per-scenario pass^k reliability rather than aggregate accuracy, and classifies every production run against the contract so that each finding routes to the asset that must change. | **[Phase-DGS: Phase-guided Dynamic Gaussian Splatting from Unsynchronized Multi-view Video](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04666v1)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Spatiotemporal scene reconstruction with Dynamic Gaussian Splatting (DGS) fundamentally depends on perfect temporal synchronization across all cameras, an assumption rarely achievable in practice. Project page: https://oc-dev74.github.io/Phase-DGS/ |

| **[Every Camera Effect, Every Time, All at Once: 4D Gaussian Ray Tracing for Physics-based Camera Effect Data Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.10759v3)** | 2026-10-31 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Current video diffusion models can generate recognizable camera-effect appearances but do not reliably follow specified physical parameters such as field of view, aperture, and readout time, partly because real dynamic videos with diverse and accurate camera-effect annotations are scarce. | **[DPGS-SLAM: Diffusion-Prior and Provenance-Aware Monocular Gaussian Splatting SLAM for Dynamic Visual Sensing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12038v1)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This paper presents DPGS-SLAM (Diffusion-Prior and Provenance-Aware Monocular Gaussian Splatting SLAM) for dense mapping in environments containing dynamic objects. Dynamic observations can contaminate camera tracking and scene reconstruction in visual SLAM (simultaneous localization and mapping). Although 3DGS (3D Gaussian splatting) enables efficient dense mapping, existing 3DGS-SLAM systems may exhibit instability when dynamic objects violate their underlying static-scene assumption. Excluding dynamic observations can reduce their influence on tracking and mapping, but may leave occluded static backgrounds missing or weakly constrained. Our proposed DPGS-SLAM is a monocular RGB SLAM framework that supports provisional representations of insufficiently observed static backgrounds through asynchronous diffusion-based background completion and provenance-aware Gaussian mapping. Dynamic regions are identified through optical-flow-based motion discovery and temporally consistent mask propagation by SAM2 (segment anything model 2). For selected keyframes, a fine-tuned diffusion inpainting model generates plausible background appearance in occluded regions, while Metric3D v2 predicts metric depth from the completed images to initialize provisional Gaussian primitives. Provenance labels distinguish generated primitives from observation-supported primitives, excluding generated content from camera-pose estimation and observed-map optimization. When previously occluded backgrounds become visible, eligible generated primitives are removed, and observation-supported primitives are independently initialized from real RGB observations and predicted depth. Experimental results demonstrated competitive camera tracking, with average ATE RMSE values of 1.2 cm on TUM RGB-D and 2.7 cm on BONN RGB-D. On StaDy4D, DPGS-SLAM improved average PSNR by 4.60 dB over WildGS-SLAM using only monocular RGB input.</p></details> |  |

| **[Branch-Extended Automatic Differentiation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08637v1)** | 2026-11-05 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We present branch-extended automatic differentiation (BEAD), a software package that provides information about inactive branches of piecewise-differentiable functions, intended for use in nonsmooth optimization algorithms. | **[A Mode-Unified, Continuous, and Biomimetic Control Strategy of a Robotic Lower Limb Prosthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19183v2)** | 2026-11-04 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Existing biomimetic lower-limb prosthesis controllers are commonly designed for discrete locomotion modes, requiring separate parameterizations for level walking, ramps, and stairs. These findings demonstrated that unified biomechanical parameterization enables a prosthesis control architecture across diverse terrain conditions while preserving key features of AB biomechanics without requiring independently tuned impedance parameter sets for each locomotion mode. | **[Ab Initio Accuracy Beyond the Cutoff: Distributed Message-Passing Equivariant Attention at Tens-of-Millions-of-Atom Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06122v1)** | 2026-11-04 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Molecular dynamics predicts molecular motion from atomic forces and trajectory integration. Density functional theory (DFT) provides first-principles forces, but its $O(N^3)$ cost limits simulations to thousands of atoms; empirical force fields scale to millions but miss bond breaking and charge transfer. We present Tessera, a distributed runtime that fixes workload shapes between rebuilds. It sorts atoms along a space-filling curve into equal-count shards, ensuring identical atom counts and tensor shapes per rank. Peer sets scale as $K^{0.5}$, not $K$. A conservative neighbor skin preserves topology across updates, enabling each rebuild interval to replay as one CUDA Graph. Fused kernels, three-level neighbor search, and overlapped All-to-All complete it. On 512 GPUs, Tessera sustains 30.7M atoms at 89.7% weak-scaling efficiency (77--90% across three per-GPU loads); per-step time rises 1.11--1.29x as machine and problem grow 16x. Graph reuse cuts full-step time $1.87\times$. Across an $8\times$ device increase, neighbor-search cost grows $1.7\times$, versus $3.0\times$ for Morton order. | **[Agentic Behavior Engineering: A Design-by-Contract Methodology for Specifying, Verifying, and Governing Enterprise AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05454v1)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Enterprise teams that deploy large language model (LLM) agents inherit a maintenance problem that traditional testing does not address. This paper presents Agentic Behavior Engineering (ABE), a platform-agnostic methodology that treats observable agent behavior, rather than the prompt or the model, as the version-controlled engineering artifact. ABE adapts Design by Contract to non-deterministic systems. A specification-only behavior contract with stable rule identifiers states what the agent must do, must not do, and when it must abstain for lack of evidence; a lightweight scenario library turns each rule into concrete situations; and every engineering artifact (tests, runtime policies, telemetry) references the contract in one direction only, so that specification and implementation can change independently without drifting apart. The method organizes the work into five responsibilities (Specify, Ground, Control, Verify, Observe), treats missing evidence and a verified negative as different things, enforces authority outside the model, gates releases on per-scenario pass^k reliability rather than aggregate accuracy, and classifies every production run against the contract so that each finding routes to the asset that must change. We illustrate the method with an executable capacity-allocation boundary suite and a constructed worked example of a product-review approval workflow.</p></details> | 30 pages, 6 figures, 5 tables. Expanded version of the author's practitioner guide "Agentic behavior engineering" in the Microsoft Azure Architecture Center (CC BY 4.0): https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/architecture/ai-ml/guide/agentic-behavior-engineering |
| **[Beyond the Bochner Method: Optimal Spectral Gaps for Block Dynamics of Spin Systems like the $O(N)$ Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05228v1)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Let $μ$ be a probability distribution on a spin system with $n$ sites and possibly infinite spin spaces. These bounds are sharp: the Gaussian system with covariance $(I-A)^{-1}$ attains equality for every choice of disjoint blocks. Our proof extends the Bochner method through an abstract inequality for orthogonal projections on Hilbert spaces. As an application, we prove that Glauber dynamics for the $O(N)$ model, with $N\geq 2$, arbitrary external fields, and couplings of either sign, has spectral gap at least $(1-\&#124;J_{\mathrm{abs}}\&#124;_{\mathrm{op}}/N)/n$, where $J$ is the interaction matrix and $J_{\mathrm{abs}}=(&#124;J_{ij}&#124;)$. We also obtain spectral bounds for swap walks and local spectral expansion of partite simplicial complexes. For complexes formed from disjoint groups of ranks in random flags of subspaces over $\mathbb F_q$, we obtain a one-sided local spectral expansion bound of $2\sqrt q/(q+1)$. This bound is independent of the number and sizes of each group, and is nontrivial for every prime power $q$. Finally, for random forests $F$ of any finite graph with arbitrary positive edge weights, we prove that \[ \Pr(e,f\in F)\le \frac{4}{3} \Pr(e\in F)\Pr(f\in F) \] for distinct edges $e,f$, improving the factor 2 supplied by general log-concavity bounds for matroid independent sets. | **[Data-driven candidate structure ensemble generation beyond the convex hull with crystALL approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02584v1)** | 2026-11-02 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Real materials consist of multiple stable or metastable states under real-world conditions, existing as statistical ergodic ensembles of configurations rather than ideal crystals. Yet, data-driven computational materials discovery increasingly biases itself toward finding "new stable structures" from ground-state DFT calculations alone. This limits our long-term ability to discover materials whose properties emerge from metastable phases, to explain experimental observations, or to model thermodynamic behavior comprehensively. Predicting ergodic sets of atomic configurations of known and hypothetical solids is a fundamental challenge in theoretical crystallography, approached via many complementary methods. We discuss this conceptually and introduce crystALL, a pragmatic and powerful data-driven approach to discovering ensembles beyond the convex hull, embracing structural diversity over stability alone, enabling robust experiment matching in cases evading traditional methods. crystALL leverages 5 million relaxed structures from OPTIMADE and MPDD to rapidly generate a comprehensive candidate set by: (1) species substitutions; (2) ML feature-space analysis with pySIPFENN; (3) representative structure selection via clustering and energy-minimization or property matching; and (4) construction of the final candidate ensemble for experimental or ab initio validation. We also uniquely address the common challenge of experimental composition uncertainties spanning many integer stoichiometries by new efficient searches across anonymized compositional ranges rather than fixed formulas. Overall, crystALL's ensemble-focused philosophy provides critical advantages over the prevailing convex-hull-searching paradigm, which often fails to account for key experimental observations and increases AI/ML discovery bias over time - akin to model collapse in LLMs retrained on iteratively generated content.</p></details> | 19 pages, 7 figures, software repository https://github.com/PhasesResearchLab/crystALL |
| **[TRUCO: A Scalable Lock-Free Algorithm for Parallel Contour Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37297v2)** | 2026-11-01 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Extracting closed pixel contours from a binary image, together with the hierarchy that records which contours enclose which, is a basic step in many computer vision pipelines, from fiducial marker detection to the vectorization of segmentation masks. The reference algorithm, by Suzuki and Abe, is inherently sequential: each contour is found by a raster scan that depends on the labels left by all previous contours, and its parent is obtained from the last contour met by the scan. This paper presents TRUCO (Threaded Raster Unrestricted Contour Ownership), a multi-core algorithm that returns exactly the output of Suzuki--Abe (the same contours, in the same order, with the same hierarchy) without locks, read-modify-write operations or a stitching phase. The method rests on two ideas. First, the image is divided into horizontal stripes, one per thread, and two rules, row-local writes and geometric contour ownership, let each thread trace complete contours independently of the others. Second, the tracing of the contours is separated from the computation of the hierarchy, which is recovered afterwards from one reference per contour; the trace therefore stores no contour numbers in the label image, which also makes it more efficient than that of the original algorithm when a single thread is used. We prove that the output is deterministic and identical to that of Suzuki--Abe, and that the algorithm is free of data races. Experiments on synthetic images, high-resolution photographs and segmentation masks, on a desktop and a server CPU, show that TRUCO scales with the number of threads and is several times faster than the fastest sequential methods, with and without the hierarchy. With a single thread, it is already faster than Suzuki--Abe.</p></details> |  |
| **[Improvise, Adapt, Overcome: An On-The-Fly Multifidelity Algorithm for Efficient Machine Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02662v2)** | 2026-10-31 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Machine learning has accelerated quantum chemistry but is hindered by the prohibitive cost of generating high fidelity training data. Multifidelity machine learning (MFML) mitigates this overhead by systematically combining abundant low fidelity data with sparse high fidelity data. In spite of its success, standard MFML schemes rely on pre-defined scaling factors to determine sparse data ratio across fidelities, often generating redundant multifidelity data resulting in a loss of efficiency. Here, we introduce an adaptive on-the-fly multifidelity framework for machine learning that autonomously determines training dataset composition. By dynamically querying training samples at each fidelity, the algorithm saturates model accuracy at lower fidelities before moving up to more expensive reference calculations. We benchmark the novel adaptive-MFML across diverse chemical properties including theoretical best estimate ab initio potential energy surfaces, golden standard coupled cluster energies, and the more chemically challenging excitation energies. In our numerical experiments we show that our adaptive algorithm reduces data generation costs by up to a factor of 20 compared to single fidelity methods and improves upon standard MFML by up to a factor of 3. The mitigation of data redundancy establishes a high-accuracy low-cost pathway for sustainable cost-aware machine learning in quantum chemistry.</p></details> | Supplementary Information added within the main text towards the end; updated several things in main text and added several things in SI |

| **[Ground-Wing: A Parametric 3D Wing-in-Ground-Effect Dataset for Benchmarking Neural Surrogate Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04571v2)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This paper presents a CFD dataset of a highly parametrized 3D wing operating in ground effect, designed for benchmarking neural surrogate models for aerodynamic prediction, using a wing shape and flow conditions typically encountered in motorsport applications. The dataset comprises 770 RANS simulations of 194 unique wing configurations, sampled across 27 airfoil profile variants and 4 distances from the ground, spanning variations in wing sweep, chord, taper, angle of attack, and tip twist. Six neural network architectures representing distinct paradigms --- convolutional (DoMINO), graph-based (X-MeshGraphNet), transformer-based (AB-UPT, GeoTransolver, GAOT), and JEPA-based (AeroJEPA) --- are evaluated on their ability to predict surface pressure coefficient and wall shear stress distributions, from which integral drag and lift coefficients are recovered. The dataset is intended to be used as a challenging benchmark for surrogate CFD models, due to the uniqueness of the ground effect being absent from other wing and airfoil datasets. The dataset is available for download at https://huggingface.co/datasets/andreacenzato/Ground-Wing</p></details> |  |
| **[Active Learning for Gaussian Process Regression Under Self-Induced Boltzmann Weights](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.10654v2)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We consider the active learning problem where the goal is to learn an unknown function with low prediction error under an unknown Boltzmann distribution induced by the function itself. We propose \texttt{AB-SID-iVAR}, a Gaussian Process-based acquisition function that approximates the intractable Bayesian target distribution in closed form while avoiding partition function estimation, and is applicable to both discrete and continuous input domains. We also analyze a Thompson sampling alternative (\texttt{TS-SID-iVAR}) as a higher variance Monte Carlo variant. Despite the unknown target, under mild conditions, we establish that the terminal prediction error vanishes with high probability, and provide a tighter average-case guarantee. We demonstrate consistent improvements over existing approaches in this setting on synthetic benchmarks and real-world PES modeling and drug discovery tasks. Our code is available at https://github.com/TsingQAQ/SIDAL</p></details> |  |

In spite of its success, standard MFML schemes rely on pre-defined scaling factors to determine sparse data ratio across fidelities, often generating redundant multifidelity data resulting in a loss of efficiency. Here, we introduce an adaptive on-the-fly multifidelity framework for machine learning that autonomously determines training dataset composition. By dynamically querying training samples at each fidelity, the algorithm saturates model accuracy at lower fidelities before moving up to more expensive reference calculations. We benchmark the novel adaptive-MFML across diverse chemical properties including theoretical best estimate ab initio potential energy surfaces, golden standard coupled cluster energies, and the more chemically challenging excitation energies. In our numerical experiments we show that our adaptive algorithm reduces data generation costs by up to a factor of 20 compared to single fidelity methods and improves upon standard MFML by up to a factor of 3. The mitigation of data redundancy establishes a high-accuracy low-cost pathway for sustainable cost-aware machine learning in quantum chemistry.</p></details> | Supplementary Information added within the main text towards the end; updated several things in main text and added several things in SI |
